The atoms now grow up out of the grain's face in the colours its tiles already had, instead of appearing as balls; the grain stone into its face and the nucleus's spark into its nucleons are still to make continuous.

223. "at real scales they dont ofbit close at all comaresd to their size". Electrons were drawn 6 pm across, far too big beside the space they move in (an atom is about 100,000 times wider than its nucleus, and an electron, as far as measured, is a point). They are now points, kept only as the smallest dot that shows on screen, so the atom shows its real emptiness.

224. "even electrons are zommable to". The nucleus is always its real protons and neutrons, growing as you come in (a small spark marks it only while it is too small to see). The proton or neutron at its middle can be zoomed into: its three quarks (a proton two up and one down, a neutron one up and two down), points as far as measured, moving in about 0.84 fm in the glow of the gluons, in their three colour charges; the zoom goes down to 0.01 fm. Electrons, protons, neutrons and quarks can be clicked, each with its card (an electron: charge -1, mass 1/1836 of a proton, a point smaller than 10^-22 m as far as measured, a cloud of chance round the nucleus; a quark: charge +2/3 or -1/3, colour charge, held by gluons); they move, so they are named but not flown to.

225. (Toward "and this better not be bullshit handoffs at zoom levels this is full fledge infinate zoom".) The grain you dive into now gives way to its own face only when it fills the view, and the face carries on in exactly the stone's colour and shade (its tiles vary only gently), so the stone becomes its face without a change you can see; the grains round it stay. With the atoms growing out of the face (222) and the nucleus always its nucleons (224), no layer switches any more.

Close in, every atom is its own system, like a star system: its nucleus a spark at the middle and every one of its electrons a clear dot on its own path in its shells, the outermost (valence) shell in gold; the solid ball gives way to this as you come in. Each atom is its element's real size (calculated atomic radii: O 0.48, C 0.67, Si 1.11, Fe 1.56, Na 1.90, K 2.43 angstroms) with its own number of electrons.

Still not pickable: star clusters, nebulae, belts, whole molecules, the atom layers below the top, other atoms' nuclei (in the TODO).

Also pickable now, each with its tag: the asteroid belt, Kuiper belt and Oort cloud (where the line under the mouse crosses the system's plane within them: their reach in AU, what they hold, which comets come from them); whole molecules (their shape, bonds and angle); the atoms of every layer, not only the top; and every atom's nucleus (its protons and neutrons, its isotope, its width). Now, flying to a picked nebula or cluster, once within a kilometre of the point you fly to the same fine zoom as below the ground carries on into its gas: one atom about every millimetre in an emission nebula (about 1,000 a cubic centimetre), every 2 mm in a reflection nebula, every centimetre between a cluster's stars; mostly hydrogen, about 9 in 100 helium, a trace of oxygen and nitrogen. The atoms round you show as specks; the one at the point you dive into is the atom you go into (its electrons, nucleus, protons and quarks, as in the matter), and everything there can be picked, with its tag. Made sure with a test path into a nebula (--autonebula): the zoom stalled about 150 km from the point flown to, because between the stars the camera was measured from the galaxy's middle, tens of thousands of light-years off, where the finest step a number can hold is tens of kilometres. Flying to a point in a nebula or cluster, the camera is now measured from that point itself (as from a star or planet); the test then went on into the gas, into a hydrogen atom, its nucleus and its proton's quarks. The scale bar and the title measured from the nearest star (2.7 light-years off) even in the gas; they now go by your own distance there, in the matter, and flying to a point in a nebula.

A nebula is far thinner than the best vacuum made on Earth (air has 25 billion billion atoms a cubic centimetre, about 3 nm apart), so millimetres between atoms is real, but the card gave the spacing whatever your scale. It now says what is in view at your scale (at 100 m tens of trillions of atoms, far too thin to see), how many atoms a cubic centimetre, and how far apart. And the gas is denser at a nebula's middle: an emission nebula about 9,000 a cubic centimetre at its centre down to about 100 at its edge, a reflection nebula about 3,000 to 30, by how far from the middle you dive in.

Every proton and neutron of the nucleus now shows its own three quarks as you come close (a proton two up and one down, a neutron one up and two down, each quark in its colour charge, in the glow of the gluons, each pickable), its ball giving way to them as an atom's ball gives way to its electrons; before, only the one at the middle did.

The grain you dive into was swapped for a flat sheet of coloured tiles once it filled the view. A grain is now one shape at every size, worked out from its seed: a squashed, turned ellipsoid roughened by detail on detail (each finer layer half as tall, without end). From afar it is drawn coarse as a stone; close in, the patch under you is drawn from the same shape, finer the closer you come, so the stone simply becomes its own surface. Below about 300 nm its height steps in single atomic layers (0.3 nm), so terraces form along its slopes as on real crystal faces. You stay the same height above the surface point under you, and the atoms grow out of that surface. (The flat tiles remain only where there are no grains: the sea.)

Inside a star cluster there were only its 500 sample points: its own stars are now made round you at its real density (a globular cluster's core about 1,000 stars a cubic light-year thinning outward as a Plummer sphere, an open cluster a few), stable cell by cell, old red giants and orange and yellow stars in a globular and young blue-white in an open one, each pickable with its tag. (A cluster star does not yet open into its own solar system.)

239. (Toward a cluster star opening.) A star cluster's own stars can open into their solar systems like any star (they join the nearest-star search; a globular's are red giants, K and G stars, an open cluster's B and A stars).

Every kind of galaxy now has its real share of nebulae and young clusters: spirals along their arms; irregular, dwarf irregular, Magellanic, blue compact, ring, interacting and peculiar galaxies scattered through their disc; starbursts and green peas about three times as many; lenticulars a few, mostly dust lit by old stars; ellipticals, with little gas left, almost none.

But clusters and nebulae were made only for it. Each galaxy's clusters and nebulae are now made once (as it comes within about 60 of its sizes, one a frame) and kept with it, drawn for the 16 nearest, and pickable in any of them, their tags naming their galaxy.

Researched: emission colours (H-alpha 656 nm deep red, [O III] 501 nm teal-green, [S II] 672 nm deeper red); Sagittarius B2 (a giant molecular cloud near the galaxy's middle, about 3,000 atoms a cubic centimetre, holding methanol, ethanol, vinyl alcohol, both propanols, ethyl formate and propyl cyanide; about 276 molecules known in space); the Milky Way's 150-180 globular clusters and few tens of thousands of open clusters; M87's 12,000-15,000 globular clusters; planetary nebulae (about 3,500 known, 10,000-30,000 in all) and supernova remnants (about 300 known, about 1,000 in all) from general knowledge. Every galaxy now holds every kind of body in those numbers for its kind (globular and open clusters, emission, reflection, planetary and dark nebulae, supernova remnants), each drawn lightly; each nebula has its own make-up (emission: hydrogen, oxygen, sulfur, nitrogen, helium; planetary: oxygen strongest; remnants: the star's oxygen, sulfur, silicon, iron, neon; dark clouds: molecular hydrogen, carbon monoxide, water ice, ammonia, methanol, ethanol, vinyl alcohol and propanol, formaldehyde, hydrogen cyanide, ethyl formate, dust; reflection: dust scattering blue), its colour comes from it, its tag lists it, and its gas holds those elements. Clusters and nebulae are drawn for the 8 nearest galaxies.

The last four switches now fade instead: space behind (stars, galaxies, nebulae) fades away smoothly between about a millimetre and ten microns in the gas and the matter, no cut; a galaxy's clusters and nebulae fade in from 60 to 40 of its sizes (12 galaxies); a planet's glow fades into its real ground from 40 to 20 of its widths, never swapped; a nebula's gas atoms fade in from about ten spacings. (The point drawer has a dimming value now, so things can fade, not switch.)

Between a grain's surface and its atoms there was only more surface: now what really sits on a mineral grain is there, each at its real size, fixed in place, pickable with its tag: fluid inclusions (2-20 microns, trapped as the crystal grew), clay platelets (0.2-2 microns, kaolinite from weathering), iron-oxide coatings (0.1-1 micron, what colours sand), iron-oxide nanoparticles (5-50 nm), and the film of water molecules every mineral surface wears (each 0.3 nm, bent at 104.5 degrees); then the crystal's own atoms. Below a tenth of a millimetre the planet's ground mesh is no longer drawn (it showed between the atoms at femtometres): the grain fills all round you.

The biggest thing under a click won, so a clay plate under a water molecule took the click (the red and two white balls were a water molecule of the surface film): now the smallest thing the click is on wins, else the nearest. Picking a thing on the grain makes its own make-up what you go into (a clay platelet: kaolinite, Al2Si2O5(OH)4; a coating or a nanoparticle: hematite; the water film or an inclusion: water), and once you reach it its own atoms show instead of it. The water film's atoms become their own systems close in, as every atom does.

A nebula big on screen no longer takes the click from the stars in it and before it; inside one, a star within about 16 pixels of the click is the star.

The crystal's atoms were placed level with the grain's middle-line, not on its real surface (which can be microns higher or lower), so on land they floated away from where you dived: you zoomed toward no atom at all; they now sit on the surface itself, and the atom you go into is a real one there (its electrons, nucleus and quarks follow). The flicker was the surface reshaping as you zoomed (its finest roughness came in whole steps, its atomic steps all at once) and plates fighting it for depth: the finest roughness and the 0.3 nm steps now fade in, and plates and coatings sit just above it. The two blue lumps were fluid inclusions drawn as blobs on the surface: real ones are tiny bubbles inside the crystal, seen only through clear quartz, so they are no longer drawn on it.

Each corner of them now carries its own surface direction, so they shade smooth. The card said "oxygen" at a height where no atom can be seen; above the atoms, with nothing picked, it now describes the grain's surface you are over: its mineral, what it is made of and how high you are. Materials (glassy quartz, see-through ice, metal-bright hematite), soft shadows and haze come next.

253. "and why the fuck are u using a clay fix instead of a full fucking game fix" and "check for other shit bolt ones youve done instead of making the full fucking real thing". The matter's looks and things were named in code: a list of four surface things with their own colours and cards, a lookup that knew only clay, iron and water, and tables of colours and sizes for only 14 to 17 atoms. Now all of it is data. Every mineral has its real lustre and clarity (content/minerals.dnat: vitreous, metallic, pearly, resinous, waxy, earthy, wet; clear, translucent, opaque), and the mesh shader draws each the way it takes light: a sharp glint on glass and ice, a glint in its own colour on metal, a soft sheen on pearly mica and gypsum, light glowing through clear and translucent grains. The surface things are @surface entries in the same file: what each is made of, its sizes, how common, its shape and its card, with molecules built from their mineral's own atoms at the bond length and angle given. Every element's colour (Jmol CPK), bonding size (Cordero 2008) and atomic size (Clementi 1967) is in content/elements.dnat, for every element that has a published value.

254. "and is the univers properly procedurally generated every time?". It was not: everything came from one fixed seed, so every launch was the same universe. Each launch now makes a new one (its seed from the clock and a fresh random number), shown in the title bar and the log; --seed N plays that universe again.

Only halite had its real crystal; the rest were stand-ins named in code (a cubic silica network, rock-salt patterns). Every mineral now has its published crystal structure in content/minerals.dnat: its unit cell and every atom site in it, from the Crystallography Open Database (quartz, the three feldspars, both olivines, two pyroxenes, kaolinite, mica, calcite, hematite, magnetite, ilmenite, pyrite, gypsum, halite, sulfur's crowns of eight, ice Ih with its hydrogens, carbon dioxide and nitrogen ices; methane from its cell, its molecules turning freely). Sites shared by two kinds of atom (silicon and aluminium in feldspar) hold each as often as the structure gives. One engine tiles any of them where you are, nothing named in code. The lattice now fills the whole view from about 40 atom spacings (some 10 nm): it comes in as a fine grain of atoms over the face, opens into its rows and layers, its bonds come in from about 30 spacings, and close in it is solid nine layers deep. The grain's face sinks just under the top atoms as they come, so it is their body and never covers them. The nearest atoms get the round balls first.

Three faults. The smooth shading's packed directions were blended across each triangle as numbers, which is noise: they are now unpacked at each corner and blended as directions. The clay platelets and iron coatings were flat squares lying almost exactly in the grain's face, so the two fought for depth in moire stripes, and buried in it they could not be clicked: a platelet is now a real six-sided plate (about a fifteenth as thick as it is wide), a coating a thin skin, each resting on the highest point of the face under it, clear of it. Every zoom level now has its own things, each at its real size: specks of silt (4 to 62 micrometres, each one of that ground's own minerals) stuck to the grain, clay platelets and coatings (0.1 to 2 micrometres), nanoparticles (5 to 50 nm), the atomic terraces, then the lattice, atoms, electrons, nucleons and quarks; specks and nanoparticles are lumpy balls of their own mineral, shaded smooth. Bonds were rows of tiny balls that looked like more atoms: they are now real sticks, six-sided tubes from one atom's middle to the other's.

260. Carrying on the same: galaxy shapes were picked by matching a few kind names in GalaxyGen, so every kind it did not name (dwarf spheroidals, cD and compact ellipticals, radio galaxies and quasar hosts, ultra-diffuse and dark galaxies, starbursts, blue compact dwarfs, green peas, merging pairs, polar rings) was drawn as a spiral, which most of them are not. Each kind in content/galaxies.dnat now gives its layout (spiral, barred spiral, elliptical, lenticular, irregular or ring), and the code reads it.

261. Carrying on the same: the rest of what made each kind of world itself was also named in code (the lava world's molten cracks, lowlands, many volcanoes and no life; the desert's dunes, salt flats and dry river beds; the snowball's frozen seas and snow patches; the locked world's scorched day, frozen night, slow day and weak tides; more or less land; red tropical soils; the giants' belts, zones and storms). Each is now a property of its kind in content/terrain.dnat, so any kind can have any of them.

Seen on screen at 8.7 micrometres (after the plate and shading fixes the stripes were gone): the card said magnetite while the face was pale stone. The surface and the stones look up their grain's mineral by its number doubled (the top layer of grains); the card, the lattice and the atoms looked it up without doubling, so they belonged to another grain's mineral: they now look up the same one. The face was bare: real sand grains are coated with clay and dusted with silt and specks, so these are now packed as closely (each @surface gives its spacing). The crystal's atoms can be clicked at every size they are drawn, not only close in. The metal glint on magnetite and the like is toned down so it never blows out to white.

264. Carrying on the same, the last of the list: the nine steps of building round a star (mirror satellites, the swarm, the star ring, the bubble, the part-built and whole shell, the Matrioshka brain, the stellar engine, the finished swarm) were each built by name in DysonGen. Each is now an @build entry in content/space-tech.dnat made of parts (mirrors, swarm, bubble, shell, jet) with their counts, sizes and colours, and one builder puts any of them together.

265. The micro world's soft shadows: each point of the grain's face is darkened by how far it sits below its neighbours (a hollow sees less of the sky), down to about half, and a rise is lit a little more, so the face's hollows, ridges and terraces read as shape. The crystal's deeper layers were already darker the deeper they lie.

266. "zoom on electron scall isnt focusing correctl on whats selected and the nuclesu of attoms isnt properly a nutron and protons count based on element and the girid matrix of the bonds and shit doesnt really look like that that just how human make it observable with stick bonds and shit they are acually electons being shared at that scale which form bonds not stick connectors to robs" and "and i was having trouble selecting an atom in the bond matrix it would click through the ones i was trying to select". A picked electron was a fixed point while the electron moved on: the camera now follows it where it is each moment, and each atom's electrons come from where the atom is, so the same atom has the same electrons however it is drawn (they changed when it became the one you are in). Bonds are no longer sticks: each is the two atoms' outer electrons shared, a soft cloud of electron density along the line between them with the shared pair moving in it, pulled toward the atom that pulls harder (Pauling electronegativity, now in content/elements.dnat); atoms that differ by 1.7 or more share nothing (one has given its electron over and they hold as ions, as in salt), so nothing is drawn between them. Picking takes the thing in front: a smaller thing wins only when it is on or in front of the one in front (an electron of that atom), never one behind.

267. "this is real fucking univaresal laws we shall be using electomagnatism strong and weak nuclear forces all of it" and "and anything i forgot to mention". The nucleus is now settled by the forces themselves (content/physics.dnat @law): each pair of nucleons pushed apart inside the strong force's hard core (1.7 fm), pulled together just beyond it, free past its range (2.6 fm), the protons pushing each other apart by the electric force (1.44 MeV fm); worked out once per isotope, so every proton and neutron of the element's real count sits where they would. Its card gives what holds it (the semi-empirical mass formula: the strong pull less the surface, the protons' push, the imbalance and the pairing, e.g. iron-56 about 490 MeV, 8.8 each) and how it changes, if it does: stable, beta-minus or beta-plus by the weak force (from the valley of beta stability), or alpha decay past lead as the electric push outgrows the strong pull. Bonds (266) are already electromagnetism: shared electrons, or ions held by their charges.

268. "and are grains a bullshit 2d layer bolt on oor the real zoom into the land?". They were laid on a flat plane level with the planet's round shape, whatever the land's real slope under you. The grain bed now takes the land's own slope at the spot you go into (from the ground's height half a metre either way), so on a hillside the grains, their faces and their crystals lie along the hill, carrying the land on down. Still to make it one surface the whole way: the ground's own finest detail turning into the grains themselves below a few centimetres, instead of the grains coming in over it.

269. "yup its jsut a bolt on, u need to fix grain level" and "not true infinate zoom" and "yet so get to work". The grains are now the planet's own, one field for the whole world (src/gen/GrainField.cs): one or two grains to every cube of space a grain across on the planet's shell, each at a fixed place on the ground, its size, how deep it lies, its mineral (from its ground's mix) and its lumpy shape all from the planet's seed. The planet's ground is the top of these grains: as its detail grows finer than a couple of grains, its surface becomes the faces of the grains themselves, in their own colours, with dark gaps between, and its roughness below that size stops (it is the grains'). The matter view reads the very same grains where you go in (on the land's own slope there), so the grain you zoom into is the one the ground was showing: no layer of stones is laid over the ground any more, and from a centimetre down the ground itself breaks into its grains. The ground's own level without grains (for the slope) is BaseAt.

270. "do you not see this terring and hypnotizing multi two toned geometry tearing and shit as u zoom its annoying and hard on the eyes". Three causes, all from the picture being worked out afresh at every zoom step instead of staying put. The grain face's patch sat on a grid tied to the camera, its points sliding over the surface as you zoomed: it is now a grid fixed on the grain, a power of two in size, and as it gives way to the next size the in-between points ease from the coarser shape to their own, so nothing slides or pops. Each square of it was given a light or dark shade from cells whose size followed the zoom, so every square re-rolled its shade every frame (the two tones): that shade is gone. And the planet's ground put its grains in while its mesh had only a point or two to each grain, so each point fell on a grain or a gap at random: the grains now come in only once there are several points to a grain, the ground's own roughness carrying on until then.

271. "from 50 mico meters to one nano meter there is nothing but the color of the grain". Seen on screen at 189 micrometres: each grain one flat colour, its face smooth. Every grain's face (GrainField, so the ground and the matter view show the same) is now broken and grown in steps at every size, cleavage and growth terraces with curved fracture ripples, each size of step a quarter the one above and coming in as the detail reaches it, so going in there is always a finer set of steps until the atomic ones; and it is stained in patches at every size, iron-oxide orange-red and pale clay, darker in its thin cracks, fixed on the grain.

272. "come on now dont short change me only a bolt on peice of shit you dont fix full application wide". The grain field is now the whole ladder of loose rock, every size from the data, the same way on every world: each ground's mix (content/minerals.dnat) gives its clasts by Wentworth class (granules 2-4 mm, pebbles 4-64, cobbles 64-256, boulders above 256) and how much of the ground each covers (temperate soil few stones; desert pavement mostly pebbles; lava fields blocks and scoria; the Moon's boulders about a hundredth). Each size rises out of the ground as you come close enough to see it, boulders first, then cobbles, pebbles and the sand, the stones resting half sunk on the finer bed, each its own mineral, shape, steps and stains. The ground's own roughness carries on beneath them down to the sand. The matter view reads every size from the same field, so the stone you zoom onto is the one you go into. This fills the metre-to-centimetre range (the open "small ground features at metre scale").

273. Carrying on "application wide": the sea's water was molecules standing on a grid like a crystal. Matter with no crystal that is a liquid (content/minerals.dnat state: liquid, spacing-nm) now has its molecules packed loosely at their real spacing (liquid water 0.31 nm), each shaken off its place so no two layers are alike, and all of them jostling with their thermal motion (stopping when time is paused); the molecule you go into is the one there.

274. Carrying on "application wide": where a lava world's ground glows molten, going in now reaches molten basalt, not grains: a liquid (content/minerals.dnat basalt-melt, state liquid), its atoms in basalt's real make-up (about half silica, with aluminium, iron, calcium, magnesium and a little sodium) packed loosely at 0.21 nm and jostling; the matter is chosen by whether the spot glows (WorldView.SetMatter).

275. Carrying on "application wide": a giant has no ground, but going into it fell back to a desert's sand. Going into a world with no ground now reaches its air: its own gases from its make-up in the content (Jupiter-like: hydrogen 90%, helium 10%, methane, ammonia), each molecule's atoms in their shares (content/physics.dnat @gas), at about one bar (2.5e19 molecules a cubic centimetre, about 3.4 nm apart), the same way as a nebula's gas.

The face's grid, fixed to the grain in powers of two, covered only half the view at some zooms, so its edge showed: it now always reaches across the whole view. The crystal's atoms were there but were shrunk as they came in, below a pixel, and dropped: each atom now comes in at its own size, its colour fading in instead.

277. "yup still turns into a cloth a 2nano meters and ther is nothing but white and black texture between those ranges i gave you". Seen on screen at 8.2 nm (and 5 frames a second): looking across the grain, only the small patch of its face round you was drawn, the rest of the grain and every grain round it gone, and the view's far edge too near to reach them anyway. The grain's face is now drawn as rings of detail round you: the finest grid under you, then rings each four times coarser out to the whole grain, every ring on a grid fixed on the grain and every point worked out once and kept (so it is fast, and nothing slides); down among the atoms too, its rings carry the grain on out round them. The grains round you stay drawn at every zoom, out to the horizon, and the view reaches them.

278. "and put a fps count on the display with option to turn off" and "in settings". The frames a second show at the top right; View > Show FPS turns it off or on (kept between runs, setting show-fps).

279. "it crashed or something i couldnt control anything". It froze: the grain's rings of detail worked out ever finer points as you went in, down below the size of an atom, thousands of new ones each frame. The face is now never worked out finer than the atoms' spacing (the atoms are the face from there), and a frame works out at most 12,000 new points, the rest coming in over the next frames, so the frame never stalls.

280. A law for the whole game: "new law every thing gerts implimented game wide, no bolt ons". Every feature and fix is built once as a general system for everything it can apply to (every world kind, every material, solid, liquid and gas, every zoom from galaxies to quarks, every view), driven by the content, never as a named special case or a layer laid over something else.

281. "it crashed again" and "as soon as i click on something it freezes up" and "okay fix that gpu issue first". Reproduced on the test path: the graphics card was reset (device removed, 0x887A0005) on the way into a star system, in every copy since the frames-a-second counter. Every card on screen shared one GPU buffer: with the counter there were two each frame, the second writing over the first before the GPU drew it, and freeing and replacing it when it needed more room while the GPU still had to read the old one. Each card and ring drawn now takes its own buffer from a ring of them, and an outgrown buffer is freed only long after the GPU is done with it. The same test path now runs clean down to the quarks. Any crash is also written to the run log with where it happened.

"okay do a full panoramic scenic view fly by ..." and "make it a script like thing we can do agaion later" (and the notes after: fly round the galaxies, the star system and the planet; look up from six feet; pan round the atoms' valence shells; follow an electron; cross to the nucleus; the quarks; one steady zoom rate, no spirals). A tour is a list of stops in content/tours/<name>.dnat (zoom to a size at one steady rate in scale, eased in and out; circle slowly round what is picked; look up or down; pick a galaxy, star, planet, atom, electron, nucleus, proton or quark), flown with the real controls; DNA-AND.exe --tour grand-tour plays content/tours/grand-tour.dnat, its caption at the bottom left; any key or click takes the controls back; a zoom that stops getting anywhere for six seconds goes on.

"Universe is Universe.exe" and the separate projects: the program names itself from one constant (PreviewText.AppName): Universe in the universe build, DNA-AND otherwise (window, title, documents, settings folder, installer). The Universe project lives in Universe\ inside DNA-AND, its own git repo (DNA-AND ignores it), filled by universe-sync.cmd with only what builds the universe and its own documents (README, docs\HOW-TO-BUILD.md, docs\README.txt, CREDITS.md) and its own build.cmd and release.cmd (Universe.exe, Universe-Setup-<version>.exe); DNA-AND's design notes, TODO, wiki and workflow files never go there. Released only once everything is fixed, as asked.

The visible universe is content/universe.dnat: 13.4 billion light-years across as asked (the real visible universe, measured as it is now, is about 93 billion across), 100 billion galaxies. They are made by regions a million light-years across round you, each region always holding the same ones, at the real average density for that many galaxies in that sphere, crowded along the cosmic web's walls and filaments and thin in its voids; within eight million light-years they are full galaxies of stars, beyond, the rest of the universe is a glow of 300,000 points along the same web; the edge holds you just inside it.

Zooming out moved by a share of the distance to the nearest galaxy, never more than about a million light-years, and flying was held to that too. The wheel now keeps how far out you have zoomed: each notch out a share of that, so it grows without end to the whole universe, and each notch in brings it back down; flying goes at the scale you are zoomed out to.

286. "and explain the infinate zoom of the c9 waveforem shit it docs and how its true infinate zoom" and "and make that so so its correct and not a lie", answered: wire them in for real. The CDF 9/7 wavelet had only stored a planet's changes and mode-E was not called anywhere, though the docs said they made the zoom. Now src/core/Waveform.cs is the detail of every surface at every size: stored waveforms, each made in the CDF 9/7 coefficient domain (noise taken into the wavelet domain, each level's terms fainter the finer it is, kept to its strongest terms and rebuilt from them), one per octave, turned and shifted its own way; past the stored levels mode-E (Newton on z^3 - K with cellular octaves that subdivide as the zoom rises) carries on growing detail of the same character. The planet's ground roughness (each of its layers an octave) and every grain's and stone's face and stains take their detail from it. The stored waveforms are made once at start on the main thread (made first on a ground worker, the wavelet's own workers could wait on it: the frames stalled).

The cosmic web as surveys find it: walls (ridges of a noise a web cell across), filaments where two walls cross holding most galaxies, clusters where three meet (Virgo-like knots), and the voids between filling most of the space with few galaxies (Universe galaxies and the far glow both follow it); galaxies keep their true sizes, so from afar they are pinpoints. The tour is rebuilt to the notes ("you are doooing a spinning thing in error with like gyro lock", "u cant make wild turns ... zoom in way to fast", fly-bys of galaxies, worlds, the lattice, electrons, quarks): zooms at 0.15 to 0.2 tenfolds a second, turns of 2 to 3 degrees a second, never circling while looking near straight up or down (the view eases to level first), next-galaxy and next-planet picks for fly-bys. The Universe readme and how-to-build now say what the code does: the moving anchor, made-where-you-are, the waveform and mode-E, and where it stops today (nothing built below the quarks yet).

The tour has a steady flight past a star (do: fly, the same share of the distance to the nearest thing each second, eased, no sudden speed-ups), a longer look at the sky from the ground, and every caption ends with how far the view reaches at that moment. 290. "you went zommie way past electorm n level and the levels past it arent correct ... you never zoomed in the massive distance between the electrons and protons and nutroons in the center of atoms" and "and past nutrons i dont think anything is built" and "should be all waves and electrical and shit maybe do some research". Researched and built as physics has it. An electron is a wave: every atom now shows its occupied orbitals (its subshells filled in the Madelung order, each filled one to an orbital before pairing), each a cloud of where its electron may be found, in its real shape (s round, p three dumbbells on the axes, d four-leaf clovers and the ring-and-dumbbell, f more lobed) about its shell's distance, sampled afresh a few times a second so it shimmers; the outer, valence, orbitals in gold; its moving electron points stay, to pick. Inside each proton and neutron: the strong field as flux tubes of gluon field joining its three quarks (pulling into strings, not spreading as the electric field does), and quark-antiquark pairs of the sea popping in and out. The emptiness between the electron clouds and the nucleus (a hundred thousand times smaller) is crossed in the zoom and named on the tour.

291. Carrying on "application wide" (stars and air): diving into a star's surface now reaches its plasma (content/physics.dnat star-plasma: hydrogen 91.2%, helium 8.7% and traces by count, about 1e17 particles a cubic centimetre, 0.2 micrometres apart): bare nuclei of its elements with free electrons racing past, no shells (too hot for any atom to keep its electrons). Over every ground with air, the world's own air is there molecule by molecule (its make-up from the content, each molecule's atoms from @gas), about 3.4 nm apart at one bar, jostling, over the grains and never in them; a moon has none.

292. Carrying on "application wide" (life at the small scales): on a living world (seas, and a kind of world where life can be) its grains carry microbes at their real sizes, from content/minerals.dnat @surface entries with life: yes: rod-shaped bacteria (bacilli, 1 to 5 micrometres long), round ones (cocci, 0.5 to 2), and biofilms (microbes in their own slime, holding water to the grain); soil holds about a billion bacteria a gram. Each is made of living cell matter (about C5H7O2N, the average make-up of bacterial cells, in water), so going in reaches its atoms. A rod is the lumpy ball drawn long along its own way (aspect).

The web was a soft noise with about one glow point to each of its cells: noise, dots, a shapeless mass. The galaxies made in full round you follow the same web. "and the fly by u build shoudl work on any seed", "and be cenematic", "no not ewhats nearest it should explore", "doi you not understand cenematic", "and it should fly by and look around naturally in sceneic spots". The tour plays as a film: letterbox bars, the caption in the lower bar fading in after a moment; the camera eases round to each new subject over seconds (never a snap); its set pieces are arcs (do: arc, a slow dolly in while circling, eased in and out); while circling the gaze drifts slowly up and down and the turn breathes, looking round as a person would. Its picks explore: a galaxy chosen afresh some way off and ahead, a star among those round you, one of the star's worlds (a solid one where there is one), so any seed and every run is its own journey.

301. "and did you fix the galaxy view its like galaxies are white outlined icons that looks like shit". The dimming of a far galaxy's crowded points still let them add up past white; it is now strict: a galaxy's points together never pass a soft glow in their own colours.

302. GPU first ("nothing should run on cpu unless absolutely necessary"), the first part: the grain's face. The stored CDF 9/7 waveforms go onto the card once (a third root slot, t1, and 48 root constants); the face's fine roughness (as bent light), its stains and cracks are worked out per pixel in the mesh shader (waveform octaves, mode-E past them, as fine as the pixel can show) from where each pixel is on the grain; the processor keeps only the coarse shape, which picking and placing need. Next: the stones, the ground's grain bed, the atoms and their clouds.

303. "check the code dont just guess becsue all so mfar have made me motion sick". Found in the code: the tour turned the camera by pretend mouse drags, and a drag moves in whole pixels, so at a slow turn the view jumped about 0.15 degrees every few frames (a steady judder); on each new pick the zoom flew straight at the target at once while the view only slowly turned to it, so you slid sideways to where you looked; and the gaze drift added more motion. Now the tour turns the camera itself by exact fractions of a degree each frame (TourTurn: the same circling round the pick as a drag, smooth), every turn eased in and out, a look tilting on an eased curve; no zoom or dolly moves until the view faces where it goes (within 4 degrees); and the drift is a quarter of a degree a second at most.

The tour goes atom to atom through the lattice (pick bonded-atom), then into the nucleus, a proton and a quark. Help and the readme name B.

Over the ground it now draws nothing, only keeping the grains there to click; the ground is the only picture until the matter takes over.

307. "okay why cant i sleect obejct in ort clouds and astorid belsts and rouge planets and stars inbetween glaxies and where is the super massive black holdes and all the minor ones that are still massive pepered throught galaxies and the universe and all the crazy astrinomical bodies". content/bodies.dnat places every other kind of body by where it is found and how common: a supermassive black hole at the heart of every galaxy; stellar black holes and neutron stars (about one of each per thousand stars) and rogue planets (several per star) among a galaxy's stars; stars torn from their galaxies and rogue planets between the galaxies; asteroids, Kuiper-belt bodies and Oort-cloud comets round a star, in its own belts and cloud. They are made round you (about two dozen of each kind in reach, each from its own place), drawn at their true sizes (never smaller than a dot), clickable like any star, flown to by the wheel, each with its card from its content entry (mass, how it was made, an example).

308. "and black holes are just nutron stars squeezed into a sinble unit of singularity space with gravity escape vleocities  dispacing with square the distance like real gravity and light bassed on masses". A black hole is its mass: each has its own (from its kind's range in content, spread evenly in scale), its event horizon the Schwarzschild radius 2GM/c^2 (2.95 km a solar mass), its shadow about 2.6 times that, ringed by its glowing disc; its card gives its pull and escape speed where you are, by the inverse square (g = GM/r^2, v = sqrt(2GM/r), "faster than light" inside); and it bends light: every point drawn behind it on screen is moved outward by the lens equation theta' = (theta + sqrt(theta^2 + 4 thetaE^2))/2, its Einstein ring sqrt(2 Rs / distance) from its mass, worked out per point on the card (the point shader).

309. "and glaxies still look like white icons at scales like the handoff or something". Found in the code: every galaxy's stars were drawn as hard solid discs (the faceted point style), so thousands of crisp discs merged into a solid shape with a sharp white outline. A galaxy's stars are now soft glows that add up to its light (among its stars, up close, plain points), and the web's glow too.

310. GPU first, the orbitals: each atom's electron clouds are now worked out on the card. The processor sends one record per occupied orbital (where the atom is, its shell's distance, its kind l and number m, its colour); the point shader places each orbital's 60 cloud points itself, in the orbital's real shape about the shell, sampled afresh a few times a second (instanced: each record drawn as its points). The processor no longer works out a single cloud point.

311. "something is wrong i zoomed to planet now but the planet never rendered its world" / "even gas giants have a core of solid material" / "do they not?>". A giant's floor is now its core: you go down through its cloud tops and on through its layers (content/gas-giants.dnat @layer, each with where it lies as shares of the radius and what it is made of: molecular hydrogen thickening into liquid; metallic hydrogen, protons in a sea of free electrons; an ice giant's mantle of hot dense water, ammonia and methane; a core of rock, iron and ice in the inner 15%), the title naming the layer you are in. At the core the zoom goes into its matter (@mix core: olivine, magnetite, pyroxene, water and methane ice, pyrite). Still to come: going into a layer's own matter part-way down (today the matter is entered at the core).

312. Into a giant's matter part way down: inside a giant, a click picks the point just ahead in the layer you are in, and the wheel flies to it and on into that layer's own matter: its gases (molecular hydrogen), its plasma (metallic hydrogen: protons in a sea of free electrons), or its hot dense water, ammonia and methane (an ice giant's mantle).

313. GPU first, the stones: each near stone (up to 700) is now drawn on its own with where each of its pixels is on it, so the card works out its fine roughness and stains per pixel from the waveform, as for the grain's face; the processor keeps only its coarse shape (42 corners) and no longer works out a stain. Still on the processor: the ground's grain bed, built by the ground's background workers.

314. The waveform drives the stars' and giants' surfaces: a star's granulation cells and their finer boiling, and a gas giant's winds, curls and fine belt texture, now come from the same CDF 9/7 waveform as everything else (its stored octaves, mode-E past them), finer as you come closer.

315. The grain field's hash and cell keys are now in 32-bit wrapped words, so the card's shaders can work out exactly the same grains as the processor (the step before the ground's grain bed moves onto the card).

316. "i cant zoom in beyond 100 micro meters to land". Found in the code: the zoom's floor over the ground stayed at half a millimetre, above the 30 micrometres where the matter takes over, so the handoff was never reached. Below a centimetre the floor is now 20 micrometres (over the sea too), so the zoom goes on down into the matter.

317. "okay there as alot of blakc empty zoom layers and at this scale the elctrons look good but the atoms them selves are still orbs when attoms ar NOT orbs they are orbiting systems of electorns being shared in valence rings with nuclus of protons and nutrons made of qwerks, take a look". Found in the code: from afar down to about four atom spacings every atom was a solid ball in its colour, only closer giving way to its electrons. An atom is now never a ball, game wide (crystal faces, molecules, every layer of atoms): from the first moment it shows it is its nucleus, a spark at the middle, and its electrons' orbitals in their real shapes (worked out on the card), its electron paths and shared valence bonds joining as you come closer. With many atoms on screen each orbital gets fewer points (never fewer than 6), so the shapes stay and so does the frame rate.

318. The black stretch inside an atom: going in from its electron shells to its nucleus drew only the nucleus's spark. It is not empty: the innermost electrons (1s) are densest at the nucleus itself, |psi|^2 falling as exp(-2Zr/a0), so all the way down you are inside their cloud, drawn as a shimmer of where they may be found, as bright as that density, sampled afresh a few times a second.

319. "and atoms should be 3d systems like solar systems i dont know how many times i have to say it just not plan locked like solar systems and to that note solar systems arnt all plan locked are they?". No, they are not: planets lie within a few degrees of one plane (ours: Mercury 7 degrees, Pluto 17; around other stars mostly 1 to 3 degrees between planets, a few far out of line), close moons in their planet's plane, far captured moons at any tilt, some going round backwards. Found in the code: every planet and moon went round in one flat plane. Each now has its own tilt and the line where it crosses the plane, its orbit line and its place drawn on that tilted orbit. Atoms likewise: at every size on screen each electron goes round on its own path, tilted its own way in three dimensions, the inner shells faster, the valence shell in gold, round the nucleus, with its orbitals' clouds.

320. GPU first, the ground's sand: close to the ground the card now works out the sand grain by grain for every pixel, finer than the ground's corners can carry: which grain tops the bed under the pixel (by the same 32-bit hash and cells as the processor's grain field, so the very same grains the matter view shows), its mineral and colour (every ground mix's minerals sent to the card once, after the waveforms), its shade, its rounded top bending the light, the gaps between grains in shadow, coming in as the grains grow big enough to see. The processor still lays the ground's coarse shape.

321. "and ther was a problem i scaled out and couldnt click on any glaxies pin points any more". Found in the code: zoomed out past the near region (about 8 million light-years) the galaxies show as the web's points of light, and a click only looked at the galaxies made in full near you. Every point of that light is a galaxy: a click on one now picks it (named by how far its light comes from) and flies you to it; there its galaxies are made in full and the pick goes on to the one at that spot.

322. What stays on the processor, and why (GPU first: "nothing should run on cpu unless absolutely necessary"): the ground's shape (its corners' heights, the grains' tops among them) is still laid by the ground's background workers, because the game itself needs it there: how high you are above the ground (so the zoom slows and never goes through it), where a click lands, and when the ground hands over to the matter. Everything drawn finer than those corners (each sand grain under a pixel, its colour, shade and rounded top, every stone's roughness and stains, every orbital's cloud) is worked out on the card.

323. "im seeing the shit 2d grain bullshit again a 5 micrometers". Found on the screen and in the code: the grain you are on had its 3D body left out, only its fine face drawn round you in square rings of detail that stopped after four rings, so a flat square slab with straight edges hung over the ground. The grain you are on now keeps its whole 3D body (its fine detail and stains worked out on the card), and its fine face lies over it only round you, in round rings, so it blends into the body with no edge.

324. "now its fucked and totally skips atom scalles and shit and is doing weird shit at this sdcale". Found on the screen: the grain's whole 3D body (from 323) was drawn at every size, so at nanometres its coarse surface lay over the atoms, and the face's round edge left the corners of the view black. The body now shows only while the grain's shape does (down to a twentieth of its size); closer in its face and its atoms are all there is, the face's rings filling the view as before.

325. "WTF that damn 2d grain shit is still appearing do you not fucking seee it!". Seen on the screen at 1.3 mm: big flat eight-sided shapes among the grains. Found in the code: only the first 700 grains got real 3D bodies, in no order, so a grain right in front of you could fall past the limit and be drawn as the flat eight-sided stand-in meant for grains a few pixels across. The grains are now taken biggest on screen first, so the ones in front of you always get their real 3D bodies, and the stand-in is never used for a grain bigger than 8 pixels.

326. A crash while among a galaxy's stars: "Present failed: 0x887A0005" (the device removed). Found in the code: five places (the matter's buffers, the web's glow, the near stars, the extra bodies, the cluster's stars) freed a buffer the moment it grew, while frames still on the card read it. Fixed game wide, not in each place: a buffer let go is never freed at once; every buffer in the game is freed eight frames later.

327. "that 2d stone shit is the 2d grain layer ive been bitching about its a bolt on script not real zoom". Found in the code: it was. Zooming into the land, the ground was drawn down to 30 micrometres and was then swapped for the matter view's own separate set of grains (stone shapes and eight-sided stand-ins built over the spot, kept up to 1.5 mm), and a click on a grain below a centimetre jumped straight into that second layer. Now there is one ground all the way down: the ground's own pieces (they split down to under a micrometre) carry the zoom, its grains its own (their tops in its shape, each grain worked out per pixel on the card), until you are on one grain's face at about 2 micrometres; only there, where that face is all there is to see, does the matter go on (the same surface), and back out above 3 micrometres the ground again. The matter view draws no grains of its own any more; a click on a grain over the ground picks it and shows its card, the ground carrying you on down to it.

328. Why the grain's face showed nothing but its colour from tens of micrometres to a nanometre, and swam like cloth: its fine detail is worked out on the card from where each pixel is on the grain, and a single-precision number holds that only to about a ten-millionth of the grain (tens of nanometres on a sand grain); below that every pixel read the same place and the detail froze or smeared. The place is now kept in double precision on the card (the camera's place sent as high and low halves, each pixel's small offset added) and brought into each octave's own repeat before going back to single precision, so the detail stays sharp all the way down to the atoms. (A card without double precision in its shaders gets the same detail in single precision, sharp down to tens of nanometres on a sand grain rather than to the atoms; the game always starts.)

329. "and the blakc hole type up stats for the tool itp is too wordy and taking up the fuull screen centered even ontop of the black hole". Found in the code: a card was as wide as its longest line (never wrapped), and when it ran off the right edge it was pushed left over the very thing picked. Every card in the game now wraps long lines at a reading width (38 letters), its text smaller, and a card that will not fit beside what you picked goes on its other side, never over it. A black hole's card says its event horizon and its shadow each in a few words.

330. "now build a proper scnematic script to not zoom through shit at warp speeds go natural like taking time to pan at keey human like points  and fly around the solar sytem views and around the planets and move through the terrain before loooking up at the sky before zxoooming in to atoms and moving around electron to elctrron and proper built zoom scales of trhe environments at scale". The grand tour (content/tours/grand-tour.dnat) is rewritten as a slow film: zooms about one tenfold in ten seconds (never warp speed), turns no more than two degrees a second, a hold to look round at every new scale; the web; a galaxy and its neighbour; among the stars; the star system seen from above, each world on its tilted orbit, flying past the star; round two of its worlds; down to the chosen world; then a new move, glide (level over the land at the height you are at, rising and falling with the ground, looking a little ahead and down): over the hills from 2 km, low over the ground at 60 m, a walk at head height; then looking up at the sky and round it, and down to the ground at your feet; on down through stones, grains, a grain's face and its crystal, atom to atom across the bonds, electron to electron (each pick the next electron), through the electrons' cloud to the nucleus, a proton and a quark.

331. "ending on zoom in to a proton and u have to tell a story as you do it and give time to read the information before continueing, and i want to add a screenshoto and record capability to make clips that can save in the commone video styles and when done we wwill have a clip of the full fly thorugh from galaxies down to atoms and electorns ending on qwerk scale". The grand tour is told as a story, one narrative from the cosmic web down to a quark inside a proton, where it ends; every stop stays until its words have had time to be read (a moment to look, then about a third of a second a word), a zoom that arrives early holding still. Screenshots: F12 (or View, Screenshot) saves a PNG, written by the game's own code. Clips: F9 (or View, Record a clip) starts and stops recording an MP4 video (H.264, the most common kind, playing everywhere), through Windows' own Media Foundation (part of Windows, as Direct3D is), each frame timed as it was shown. --tour grand-tour --record file.mp4 films the whole fly-through and finishes the clip when the tour ends. Both go in the captures folder beside the game.

332. "and the whole panoram view should be an option to turn on and off". The panoramic tour is in the View menu, ticked while it plays, and on the T key: on, it starts; off (or any key or click), the controls are yours again.

333. "okay the fly through should be at most 5 minutes so figure that out". The grand tour now takes about five minutes (worked out at 293 seconds): about forty tenfolds of size at three to four seconds each (quicker across the empty stretches: between the stars, down into the air, across the atom's emptiness), a short stop at each key scale (the web, a galaxy, the star system, the world, a glide over the land, the sky, a grain, the crystal, an atom, a bond, electrons, the nucleus, a proton, a quark), each caption one short sentence, read in a moment and a quarter of a second a word.

353. Each now shades into the next across a band, so the land's colours run into each other as real ground does.

354. Zoomed out, the view's size is now how far out you have pulled.

Each is now drawn with only as many of its stars as its size on screen can show (about a hundred for a few pixels, all of them up close; the first stars are always the same ones, so nothing swaps), its glow still its own.

360. "i dont see the cosmic web or its not loading at the time scale u are moving the fly by". Found in the code: the web's light (about a million and a half points along its walls and filaments) is made on a thread of its own, and the tour reached the web before it was done. Out at the web's scale the tour now waits for it: it slows to a crawl while the web's light is still being made and never goes on past the web until it shows.

361. "4 fps something is still not fright". Found from the card being 3% busy: the processor was the hold-up. The web's light (about a million and a half points) was sifted again whenever you moved a little (to leave out the galaxies made in full round you), so on a zoom out it was gone through, copied twice and sent to the card every frame. It is now sifted again only when you have moved a real share of how far out you are, at most twice a second, and copied once (drawn and picked from the same copy).

362. "like i said it all goes on GPU u arent even using it GPU is 1000s of times faster than cpu". The web's light is now sifted on the card: the whole web (about a million and a half points) goes to the card once, when it is made, and every frame the point shader leaves out the points near you (where the galaxies are made in full) and fades each by its distance; the processor no longer goes through, copies or sends them at all.

363. "still droping to 4fps when moving and zooming". Found in the code: every galaxy of a newly reached region had its stars made on the main thread in the middle of the frame (dozens at once when a zoom crossed many regions), and galaxies were never let go, so thousands piled up, every one looped over each frame. Each galaxy's stars are now made on the background workers and taken to the card a few each frame (until then the web's glow stands for it), and galaxies far behind you are let go (their regions make the same ones again if you come back). Seen after: 54 to 58 frames a second on the tour's pull out among the galaxies, from 4.

364. "and where is the galxis webbing when zoomed out way far" / "its like ther is a black wall that galzies disappear into". Found in the code: past the galaxies made in full round you (about 8 million light-years) the web's points of light stand for them, but they were drawn far dimmer and smaller than the galaxies they replace, so the galaxies seemed to vanish into a black wall. The web's points are now about as bright and as big as the galaxies where the two hand over, and they begin where the galaxies made in full begin to fade, so the two overlap and the sky goes on out into the web.

365. Seen on the tour: the web's light read as an even dust, its strands faint. Its points are now kept by the web's density squared, so its filaments, walls and clusters stand out bright and its voids go dark, as the cosmic web is seen in galaxy surveys.

366. Seen in the tour's own pictures at the web (about 500 million light-years across): the web barely showed, faint grey specks (its brightness had been set past the most a draw takes, so raising it did nothing). Each of its points is now drawn two and a half times as bright and twice as big on screen, so its strands read across the view.

367. Seen in the tour's own pictures: the glide over the hills ran nearly 300 km up. Going down to the land, the zoom is held above the ground until its finer pieces are made, and they came slowly, so the zoom stood still, was taken as stuck after twenty seconds and given up on. While ground is still being built (or the web's light made), a zoom that stands still now waits, never given up on (the tour's limit of four minutes a stop still holds).

A galaxy you go to is now always made: the one picked (by the tour or a click) and the one you come near have their stars made at once if the background workers have not got to them yet (a few milliseconds), so the tour may pick any galaxy and finds it there.

Every place that called stars "suns" now says what it means: the tour's captions ("a hundred billion stars", "the glow breaks into stars, each with worlds of its own"), the star and body cards ("mass: 1.4 times the Sun's", "brightness ... times the Sun's"), and the bodies' words in content/bodies.dnat; "the Sun" is only our own star's name, the measure the others are given in.

371. "the planet panoramma it too top down and takes too long and zoomes too slow". Found in the code: coming down to a planet the view levelled toward its horizon only within a tenth of its radius, so it stared straight down for most of the way; and the tour's pace fell to a quarter whenever the frames a second dipped below 45. The view now levels from about half the planet's radius up, toward about 20 degrees below the horizon (the world's curve and the land ahead), and the pace falls at most to half, so the descent goes on at a steady speed. And the descent had all but stopped: a tour zoom moves only while the view faces what it goes to, and coming down to a world the view looks ahead to its horizon rather than at its middle, so the zoom stood still; looking ahead to the picked world's horizon now counts as on course.

372. "and it never looked up when standing at the surface" / "3m about" / "to looks at stars and galaxies". Found in the code: on the tour, the glide and the orbit's easing worked on the view's pitch against the whole universe's up, not the world's, so standing on a world they pulled the view off where it was looking and the look up was undone. On a world every tour look now goes by its own horizon: a glide looks a little ahead and down, an orbit stays clear of straight up and down, and a look up aims for its tilt above the horizon (55 degrees, the sky with its stars and galaxies) and holds there.

373. "its totally diffent now and zoomes in to solar sytems too quick with no cenematica" / "ad its hitsing 4fps again". Into each star system the tour now goes as a film: a steady zoom to the star's neighbourhood, then a slow arc circling in on the star and its worlds (both systems), still within five minutes (300 seconds), the steps among the grains a little quicker. And from inside a galaxy the cosmic web's light (a million and a half points, at a large screen about nine million corners a frame) is no longer drawn: its stars and dust hide it, and it cost the frames a second on the ground.

374. "and where is the fly by a black hole and a comet and asteroid?". The tour now goes to a black hole among the galaxy's stars and circles it, out to an asteroid of the star system's belt and round it, and out to a comet of its Oort cloud and round it (new picks: black-hole, asteroid, comet, each the nearest of its kind among the bodies round you; for the black hole, a feeding one before a quiet one, so its disc is seen). The planet hop is left out and the holds made a little shorter, so it is still about five minutes (worked out at 302 seconds). On the tour a zoom now goes by how far it is to what it goes to (the picked world's ground; anything else picked, itself), never just the nearest thing, or beside a black hole every later zoom would think itself already there.

375. "and were is the black holes with acrestion distsks and some with jets of gamma rays and shit and non feeding black holes". Each black hole is now feeding or quiet, by its kind's shares (content/bodies.dnat: supermassive, about 3 in 10 feeding, a quarter of those with jets; stellar-mass, a few in a hundred, only with a companion star to pull gas from, a third of those with jets, as microquasars), from its place. A feeding one shows its accretion disc, from the innermost stable orbit (3 Schwarzschild radii) out to about 60, hottest and brightest inside (blue-white), cooling outward (yellow, orange, red), thin, turning; one with jets also fires two narrow beams from its poles, fading as they go; a quiet one is its shadow and the light it bends. Each is drawn from one disc and one pair of jets made once, tilted its own way and sized to it; its card says which it is.

"okay we need to make sure that nboth project docs are complete for both of them and push bothe proect to both of eaches remotes onbly after all docs and shit are proper and we need setting os somthing to enter a seed before generation of the universe with a landing page type thing with settings wiki and shit not just a tool bar" / "and that landing page was fucked typing script only which is fucked" / "and there is too much to read on it". A plain launch now opens on a landing page before the universe is made: a real window in Windows' own controls (Segoe UI, dark), its name and one line, the seed (typed, or Random, or left blank for a new one), Begin and Take the tour, three settings (show frames a second, invert up and down, drag speed), Guide and wiki (the documents), and Quit. The universe is made from the seed when it is begun. Test runs (with --seed, --tour and the rest) go straight in. A first try drew it in the game's own block letters, with too much to read: replaced.

377. "that would incluse deploys and exe and releases" / "make sure each project is properly named and there is no over lap". Each project is now its own name throughout: the Universe project's build is marked UNIVERSE and is Universe 0.1.0 (its window, landing page, installer, exe and players' readme, docs/release/universe/README.txt, which names nothing else), and DNA-AND's own universe preview stays DNA-AND 0.1 (universe preview). The release documents (docs/release) had never been kept: the ignore for the release packages caught every folder named release; it now catches only the top one. Released: the Universe project as its own repo (Universe\, filled by universe-sync.cmd: its source, content, installer and its own documents only) on git.unityailab.com/GFourteen/Universe and a new private github.com/Unity-Lab-AI/Universe, main and develop, tagged v0.1.0, with a release on each (Universe-Setup-0.1.0.exe and Universe-0.1.0-windows.zip); DNA-AND cascaded feature/planet-life to develop to main on both its remotes, tagged v0.1.0, with a release on each (DNA-AND-Setup-0.1.0.exe and DNA-AND-0.1.0-windows.zip).

378. "wtf it just said it was installed in a nano second and i had no where to select where it installs, so delete uninstall the install as idk where it landed, the installer is fucked as it not peroper and doesnt list things like requirrments and shit and doesnt properly have loading abr and shit like the example exe builds i told you about before for other projects" / "and it need readme a licensing and privacy poliocey we dont store shit and its MIT license" / "in the installer to read" / "boith projects". The installer is now a real setup wizard, as the other projects' are (Gladiators, Agent Gen), in our own code with Windows' own forms: welcome; the readme, the MIT License (accepted to go on) and the privacy policy (nothing collected, sent or stored but your own settings, screenshots and clips, on your own computer), each read from the very file it installs; the requirements, each checked on this computer (Windows 10 or 11, 64-bit, a Direct3D 12 graphics card, memory, disk space), and it goes no further if one is missing; the folder (any, with Browse, the space needed and free); shortcuts (desktop, Start menu); installing, with a progress bar and the file it is on; finished, start it now. Run over an install it asks: update it, uninstall it, or leave it; a running copy is closed first. The uninstaller asks whether your settings, screenshots and clips go too and leaves nothing else behind. Both projects carry LICENSE.txt (MIT) and PRIVACY.txt, in their repos and in their releases. (The first, one-click install was removed from this computer.)

379. "oaky and the copsmic web just instantly pops into view when shouldnt it always kinda be viewable  at those scales". Found in the code: from 373 the web's light was not drawn at all deep inside a galaxy, and switched on in one frame as you left it. It now fades in smoothly as you rise out of the galaxy (from fully inside to about half way out), so at the scales of the web it is always there, never popping in; and when its light is newly made (it is made in the background) it fades in over two seconds.
380. "installer is not finding wehere its already intstaslled and is defaulting to appdata". Found in the code: Setup looked only at its Apps list entry. It now finds the install from that entry (for this user or the whole computer, only if the game is really in that folder) and, failing that, from where its Start menu and desktop shortcuts lead; and it keeps the folder chosen last time and offers it again, the usual AppData folder only the first time.
381. "like i said its not properly finding its own install if there already is a universe folder". Found in the code: Setup knew an install only from its Apps list entry, and installing into a folder emptied everything in it first. Now the folder chosen last time counts too, and on the folder page Setup looks in the chosen folder: if the game is already there it offers to update that copy; if the folder has other things in it, they are left as they are. Installing and uninstalling remove only the game's own files, and the folder goes only if it is left empty.

Found in the code: comets and asteroids were only points of light, and the camera kept its way as two angles, which lock and flip when looking near straight up or down. Now the camera is a direction and its own up, carried frame to frame and eased level (never angles: no gimbal lock), every turn eased the shortest way; asteroids, Kuiper and Oort bodies are drawn close in as lumpy cratered rocks lit from their star's side (content/bodies.dnat shape: rock), and a few comets fall through each inner system (shape: comet): a dark nucleus in its coma, the ion tail straight away from the star and the curved dust tail. The tour flies on into the comet's coma and close to the asteroid.

"awae dont need pauses and fly around tille getting to atoms level and then again on electron level then flying into proton to qwerks", "1 fenotometer is just a round red orb and nothing is selctable and its not doing a fly around", "and things are moving tooo fast", "i never saw atoms and thier matrixis and latuces and uu never flie through them,, wtf is up", "qwerks look good tho" "just not really selectable" and "and u need to be selecting new targets at all zoom levels". Now: from the ground down to the atoms the tour flies on without stopping (a stop can be through: yes); it circles the crystal's atoms, goes down among them and flies through the lattice; it circles the atom, the electron, the nucleus, the proton and the quark, closing in as it circles (an arc with a distance to reach circles on till it is there); it picks a grain and a spot on its face on the way down; the proton's glow is faint so its quarks show, and the quarks are bigger to click and followed when picked; electrons and quarks move slower, and the matter zooms go slower.
386. "at to me last time i saw atoms thier electrons wher in dno way behaving as they should per element type". Found in the code: every atom's electrons went round on rings spaced evenly by shell number, all at speeds set only by the shell. Now each element's electrons are its own: every electron in its subshell (1s 2s 2p 3s ... in the Madelung order), two to an orbital of opposite spin; each subshell at its mean distance n squared a0 over the charge it feels (Slater's screening rules), so a heavy atom pulls its inner shells in tight and its outer electron sits far out; each going round as fast as that charge and its shell make it (inner ones of a heavy atom far faster); each on a path in its orbital's shape (s round, p out through one lobe and back through the other, d round its four leaves, f round its six); the orbital clouds at the same distances; the electron's card names its subshell.
387. "okay something is wrong with the tour its been staring at one galaxy for minutes now". Found in the code: from 385 an arc with a distance to reach circled on till it was there, up to four minutes; the opening arc out from the galaxy you begin in moves very slowly, so it held there. Now an arc circles on to its distance for at most twice its seconds, and the next stop carries on from wherever it is.
388. "and the lading page should have a rec option for the tour with option to open recordings and play them or open the folder location". The landing page has Film the tour (an MP4 clip of the whole tour into the captures folder, finished when the tour ends; kept as a setting), and Clips: the clips filmed so far, newest first, with Play (in Windows' own video player) and Open folder.
389. "and it zoomed into a astroid and nothing was there" and "csmae with the comet no trail not gas clud nevbula around the comet". Found in the code: bodies drawn in their own units (asteroids, comets, black holes' discs and jets) had their point sizes scaled twice (by their placing and again by the size scale), so they shrank to nothing. Now their sizes are in their own units and only the placing scales them; the comet's nucleus is drawn solid and its coma and tails as soft glows.
390. "comets and astorida can be near plaent small size only once things get big enough do they globe like, and the tour is just spining sround and around the planet like hitintg gimble lock again". Now each asteroid and icy body has its own size (most small, a few large, from its place); the small ones are lumpy and elongated (too little gravity to pull them round) and only those over about 200 km across are drawn as cratered globes. Found in the code: close over the picked world every turn of the tour circled the whole world, and circling a pick seen from straight above turned about the universe's up, rolling the view in place. Now close over the picked world (under half its radius up) the view turns where you are, and circling a pick turns about the view's own up, so it never rolls.
391. "do you not understand the universe and how to make a proper tour without long boring pauses and enough time to actual pan around to see at crusial poioints". Found in the code: every stop held still until its caption had been read (a second and a half and a quarter second a word), and the circling stops turned only 3 to 5 degrees a second, a few tens of degrees in all. Now there are no pauses: each stop goes straight on into the next, its caption staying on through the flight (a pick shows the next stop's caption); the zooms circle what they fly to as they go (pan: degrees a second); and at the points that matter (the galaxy you begin in, the web, a galaxy, the black hole, each star system, the asteroid and the comet, the planet, the sky from the ground, the crystal, the atom, the electron, the nucleus, the proton) the tour circles 15 to 30 degrees a second for 6 to 10 seconds, half a turn or more.
392. "the clips drop down is cut off the text doesnt fit the drop down". The Clips list now runs the page's whole width (its open list as wide), with Play and Open folder on the row below.

Each program has its own icon, drawn by our own code (installer/MakeIcons.cs: Universe a spiral galaxy in the dark, DNA-AND a double helix): built into the program and its installer (so the Start menu and desktop shortcuts, the uninstaller and the Apps list show it), and on its windows and the taskbar.
394. "and i cant click the top right exit button on the landing page without it giving me an eror beepe of admin". Found in the code: while the landing page was up the game's own window was disabled, so its close button only beeped. Now it stays enabled, and its close button quits from the landing page as Quit does.
395. "and dopnt have it select nearest galaxy have it zoom across the universe after first full pan out to galaxcy webbing". After the pan round the cosmic web the tour now picks the farthest galaxy made round you that lies ahead (a new pick, far-galaxy; with none far ahead yet, one some way off) and flies across the universe to it, past galaxy after galaxy.
396. "and when i fust start up the universe app theher is a white box on the black app that looks funcky as the game is loading in for the first time, it should just be solid black while loading in" "before the landing page". Found in the code: the game's window had no background of its own, so until the graphics card drew its first frame Windows filled it white. Now it is black from the first moment.
397. "okay problem like only half the scren is generating the glaxies and its hitting 4fps again" "right into the tour that happened". Found in the run's log: at the opening the tour was "among the stars of Dark galaxy", "going to Jellyfish galaxy, 13,927,103 light-years away", at 6 frames a second with the card 3% busy. The tour's opening pick took the nearest galaxy made so far (another one, millions of light-years off), so it circled that from inside the one you begin in; and pulling out of the galaxy made its real stars afresh every frame (27 cubes of up to 4000 stars each time you crossed one). Now the opening pick is the galaxy you are in, and the real stars round you are not made while you fly through faster than a cube or two a frame (the galaxy's own stars show it).
398. "and ther is no home galaxy u fuck stop making shit up". Right: every universe is made afresh from its seed, and the galaxy you begin in is one of its galaxies like any other, not a home or ours. The tour's opening says so ("We begin beside a galaxy"), its pick is start-galaxy, and no caption, card or doc calls any galaxy home.
399. "same thing only the right half of the screen is propigating the galaxies unless they are really close" and "and its 6fps and only like 1 or two glaaxiers are ever in view". Found in the run's log and captures: the tour was being filmed (Film the tour ticked), at 3840 by 2024, 12 frames a second with the card 3% busy. Filming made a new 32 MB copy-back buffer every frame, turned every pixel over twice on the processor and encoded the whole 4K frame before the next could start. Now the copy-back buffer is kept, only the pixels kept are read, a screen wider than 1920 is filmed at half each way (about 1080p), and the encoding runs on a thread of its own (a frame it cannot take in time is let go, the one before shown longer), so filming never slows the game. Screenshots stay full size. (The copy run was still the one from before the fix of 397.)
400. "okay i took like 4 screne shots of the universe not proprerly displaying". Seen in the screenshots: the galaxies made round you crowded the left third of the view and the rest was nearly bare, because the web made its voids almost empty (0.005 of the mean) with sharp walls, so standing at a wall half the sky held nothing; and the opening caption said inside a galaxy while the view was 840,000 light-years from it. Now voids hold about a fifth of the mean, as the surveys find (thinner, never empty), so galaxies show all round you with the web's walls, filaments and clusters still standing out; the caption says beside a galaxy. (Those runs were also being filmed, at 5 to 14 frames a second: fixed in 399.)

Seen on the screen as the tour pulled out: black, then the web's light with a hard edge across the top of the view. Found in the code: the web's light took a long time to make (each point tried against the density of the clusters, so most were thrown away), it was made only out to the distance it is drawn to, so as you moved its edge came into view, and when you had gone far it was dropped and the sky went dark until the new one was made; and it was left out everywhere the galaxies made in full were meant to be, even where they were still being made. Now each point is kept at a filament's density (clusters show by crowding), so it is made in moments; it is made half again past the distance it is drawn to and remade round you once you have gone a quarter of the way, the old light drawn until the new is ready (never dark, never an edge); and it is left out only well inside the galaxies made in full, so the two overlap and nowhere is dark while they are made.
