Found in the code: the nearest drawing distance was a thousandth of the nearest thing, and asteroids, comets and black holes were not counted, so near one of them it was 100,000 km out and cut them (and the comet's coma and the near part of its tails) away entirely. Now the bodies drawn close round you count, so the nearest drawing distance is always in front of them.

The opening and the circling of a galaxy crept in (a twentieth and three hundredths of a tenfold a second); now they close in at a fifth and a seventh. And while the tour is held back because what is ahead is still being made (the web's light, the ground under heavy building), it never stands still: it keeps circling what it shows, and its caption says "(making what is ahead...)".

Now only the flights among the galaxies and stars pan; the zooms down to a world, its land, its grains and its matter, and to the asteroid and the comet, go straight in; and while the tour waits it circles only out among the galaxies, never by a world. (The copy run was from before 402 and 403.)
405. "dnd  5 fps so u arnt proply not doing other shit and only the view window". Seen on the screen: 11 micrometres over a grain of ice, the graphics card a quarter busy, 5 frames a second. Found in the code: the grain's face was worked out and built as rings of up to 160 by 160 points round you every frame, all the way round, whether in view or behind you. Now a point of the face is worked out only if it is in view, and only the face in view is built and drawn.

Found in the code: the tour's pace was held to the frame rate (half pace at 20 frames a second or less) and to the ground still being built, even down in the matter where nothing is waited for, and the zooms from the grain to its face and its atoms went a quarter of a tenfold a second. Now in the matter the tour goes at its full pace, and those zooms go nearly twice as fast (from a millimetre to the atoms in well under half a minute).

Found in the tour: the pull out circled the galaxy it left as it went (6 degrees a second at hundreds of millions of light-years: whole stretches of galaxies swept sideways past the view), and the circling of the web turned 20 degrees a second; and the web's light faded within a billion light-years. Now the pull out goes straight back, a little slower, the galaxies falling away in front of you; the web turns slowly (5 degrees a second); and the web's light is brighter and fades over two billion light-years, drawn out to six and made round you out to almost seven, so the far web shows.
408. "put on ur movieaehat and make it proper dynamically and prcedural like". The tour now has a director: every shot is made afresh each viewing, so no two tours are the same. Each circling shot picks its own way round (left or right) and its own speed (three quarters to one and a quarter of the script's), and out in space a slow crane up or down as it goes (on a world the gaze only drifts); the world it goes down to is chosen by eye (a world with seas and air most of all, then any solid world, by chance among them); the galaxy it crosses the universe to is another kind than the last where it can be.
409. "and how do i bring the landing page back up is it a drop down from the menu toolbars?". The menu bar has Start page, beside Exit: the program opens afresh on its landing page (a new seed, the tour, the clips) and the one you are in closes (a clip being filmed is finished first). The Help menu's About names the program itself (Universe, or DNA-AND).
410. "tour is still jsut side panning for 50 million miles as galaxies fly by in darkness". Found in the code: every turn of the tour with something picked circled it, however far: circling a galaxy hundreds of millions of light-years off swept the camera sideways across all of that space (the turn round the web, and the flight across the universe, which also circled as it went), the galaxies near the camera streaming past. Now, as a film would, the tour circles only what is close and fills the view (within twelve of its own sizes, or three times the nearest thing); farther off it turns where it is, a pan across the scene; and the flight across the universe goes straight.
411. "i told you to put on your movie making hat" and "stop it with these shit edits and do the full fucking thing correctly". The tour out in space is now filmed with real shots, not the mouse wheel and turns. A shot (do: shot) frames what is picked by its own size: the camera moves in the subject's own frame, from where it is to how far it should be (in the subject's own sizes, to-radii, or in km), sweeping round it (sweep, degrees) and rising or sinking (rise, degrees), always facing it; the distance glides evenly in scale and every move is eased in and out; and a shot lasts long enough that the scale never changes faster than its max-rate (a third to a half of a tenfold a second), so nothing ever streams past. The subject is followed as it moves (worlds on their orbits). The space half of the tour is one film in these shots: an establishing circle round the galaxy you begin beside, a pull straight back to the web, a slow pan across it, a flight across the universe to a far galaxy of another kind, a circle round it, down to the black hole at its heart and round it, out to a star and round its worlds, the asteroid and the comet framed and circled, another star, and a world framed and circled before the fall to its land (the land, the grains and the matter as before). The director still varies each shot's way round every viewing.

Found in the code: the web's light took the better part of a minute to make (1.5 million points, each kept only one time in fifty), and the tour held still in the dark waiting for it; and the galaxies made round you went to the card only six a frame. Now each point is kept one time in four (a wall's density and a little more keeps every point: filaments and clusters still show by crowding, voids two hundred times thinner), at most 900,000 of them, so the web's light is made in a few seconds; and two dozen galaxies go to the card a frame.

418. "astroid it zoomed into was a ghost like apporition and not properly stone or rock or other elements". The asteroids, icy bodies and a comet's nucleus were clouds of glowing points (they add up, so they read as a see-through ghost). Now each is solid stone: a sphere of 5,120 triangles pushed out and in by broad lumps (the small ones elongated, those over 200 km across a near globe), pitted by craters with raised rims and grit, drawn as lit stone with flat facets, lit from its star's side (its night side half lit, as everywhere). A comet's nucleus is near-black stone, in its coma and tails as before.
419. "on planet drop in its hit lows of 7fps". Seen on the screen: the processor 63% busy across all sixteen cores as the ground is built. Found in the code: the background workers were one for every core, at the same priority as the game's own frame, so while the ground was being built the frame waited for a core. Now there are two fewer workers than cores, below the frame's priority, so the frame always has its own. (That run was also filming the tour: Film the tour is still ticked.)
420. "andf ther should be an options to turn off day night cycle to be only day", "and days need to be when the star is actuall on that side of planet" and "as polanets rotate and spin". Found in the code: the world's ground was lit by its starlight turned by its whole spin since the start of time, while the sky was drawn as it is, so the day on the ground was not where its star was in the sky. Now when you come to a world its ground is lit from where its star really is; from then on its ground is held still under you and the sky turns round the world's middle by how far it has spun (the star rises and sets, the stars wheel over), the light on the ground turning with it, so day is always where the star is. And Time > Always day: the star stays high over wherever you are (sixty degrees up, so the ground still shows its shape), no night on the worlds.
421. "it stopped at 500 nano metter looking like a freeze". Found in the code: from 414 the tour flew in among the grains only once the point you dive into was over what was picked, and that counted the height too, which on a spot of the grain's face never matched, so it waited for ever. Now it counts only how far across, and never waits more than a few seconds.
422. "and 2 nano meter is jsut static", "its all jsaut stat from nanaometers to pico" "static". Seen on the screen at 675 picometres: golden clouds round every atom in view, each sampled afresh six times a second, so the whole view flickered like a television's static. Now the orbitals' clouds are steady (sampled once, the electrons moving on their paths are what moves) and are drawn only for the atom you are at; the atoms round it show their nucleus and their electrons on their orbital paths, so the crystal reads as atoms, each its own little system.
tf1 ...} ... in the viewer". Found in the code: each page went to the viewer's rich-text box as plain window text, leaving the box to tell for itself that it was formatted text, and it took the credits page as words, showing its formatting raw. Every page now goes in said outright to be formatted text (EM_SETTEXTEX).
423. "it pased too long at 106 light years and as u can see its focused on a glaxy and the web is still all viewable interseting with stars of the galaxy clasdhing too much", "the tour crashed", "like i said it froze". Found in the code: the tour ended at any key reaching the window, Shift, Ctrl, Alt and the Windows keys included, so a screenshot (Win+Shift+S) or Alt+Tab ended it and left the camera standing still; and the web's points were drawn from 3.2 million light-years round you while the galaxies made in full are drawn to 12 million, both at once over the galaxy in view. Now only a deliberate click, wheel or key counts (never a modifier, a shortcut held with Alt, Ctrl or a Windows key, or the click that brings the window to the front), and the web fades in exactly where the galaxies made in full fade out.
424. "like it should say mouse movement tour paused if done and shit" / "with restart". A tour you take the controls from pauses and says so, and why ("you clicked", "you turned the mouse wheel", "you pressed the X key"), with where it was; T carries it on from that stop, R starts it again, Backspace closes the note. At its end it says the tour is over and offers it again. A filmed tour skips the paused time and finishes its clip at the end.
425. "make sure world view isnt labeld wrong and should actually be universeview". The one camera through every scale is `UniverseView` (was WorldView); the old 36-galaxy opening view of the DNA-AND journey is `GalaxiesView` (was UniverseView); the title bar no longer says "one world".
426. "and the readsmes and shit need proper formating and shit it just looks like a shit text files with no actual style of formating or layout and shit" / "the in game all doc s". About, Readme, How it works and Credits were plain text in the code; they are now formatted documents (`docs/release/IN-GAME-*.md`, `docs/release/universe/CREDITS.md`) built into the program. The viewer draws real tables with borders and a shaded header row fitted to the window, links that show their words and open in the browser, italic, quotes in a shaded box, dividers, headings with a rule under them, and page margins.
427. "and the project readme has the same fucking problem not beiung fun to read like all docs public versions for the public" / "doics need update to that last info i said". The Universe readme, build guide, credits and installed README.txt rewritten to be fun to read and laid out, and to say what the project is: a universe to fly through and a toolkit to build universes for games and simulations.
428. "and we need how to docs on how to build in the universe from adding more planet types to features and all kinds of shit so its a tools to build universes of simulations and games". New guide, Build your own universe (`docs/release/UNIVERSE-GUIDE.md`, also in Help): templates, the content format, adding kinds of worlds, stars and galaxies, changing the whole universe, minerals and matter, writing tours, adding features in code, shipping.
429. "thats a problem they were all suppose to be dynamic and procedurally genreated templet like things not hard code" / "yeah day length is the spin of the planet". Found in the code: which kinds of world a system made, their sizes, moons, colours, days, cards and the tour's choice among them were named in code. Now every kind in `content/terrain.dnat` is a template with its rules for where it forms; any number or colour may be a range, and each world takes its own value from its seed. A world's day is one turn of its spin, from its template's `day-hours`. The same seeds still make the same kinds of world where they did; ocean worlds and tidally locked worlds round dim red stars now form as well.
430. "and make sure ther are no abrupt turns or wild pans as it cant look shooppy and needs to be smooth and continueous". Found in the code: each film shot turned the view to its subject at once; the circling shot began at 40% of its turn from a standstill; the tour's zoom switched fully on or off as the view came within an angle of its subject; the waiting turn started at 10 degrees a second at once. Now the look glides to the subject over the shot's first part, every turn and zoom eases in and out, and the zoom slows and speeds smoothly with how on course the view is.
431. "still open shit u just said so why did u halt" / "planets can have rings too". The audit carried on through every generator. Found in the code, each now a trait of its template: a galaxy's arms, young clusters and nebulae, old clusters, how much it holds and whether its bodies lie on arms or swarm in a ball were chosen by words in its kind's id (galaxies.dnat: arms, arm-spread, star-forming-rate, structure-size, globular-clusters, bodies-on-arms, bodies-spread, each galaxy its own value in a range); rings were made only for ids gas-giant and ice-giant and never drawn in the universe at all (terrain.dnat: rings-chance and the rings' traits for any kind of world, rocky worlds now and then, drawn round the world where it is); volcano counts, sea-floor volcanoes and rivers were a table of names (volcano-count, seafloor-volcanoes, rivers); a giant's inner layers were chosen by the name ice-giant (gas-giants.dnat in:); a Dyson shell's land was told by its name (now by being a habitable shell); a star system's number of worlds, first orbit, spacing and the share of hot-Jupiter systems were numbers in code (solar-systems.dnat: planets, first-orbit-au, first-orbit-spread, scales-with-star, spacing, share-in-universe). Every universe made before is made the same, but for the new rings.
432. "what about physics and the natural laws like strong nuclear and weak forces electromagnaism and gravity where all all those docs". Found in the code: gravity's pull (G times one solar mass), the speed of light and Earth's pull were numbers written in the code; now they are laws in content/physics.dnat (@law gravity, @law light) read by one place (`Physics`), from which every pull, escape speed and black-hole horizon is worked out. The guide gains The laws of nature: each of the four forces, what it does in the universe and where its numbers are (gravity: Kepler orbits, pull and escape speed, horizons and bent light; electromagnetism: electron shells and orbitals, bonds, star colours by Planck's law, the protons' push; the strong force: the nucleus packed with its hard core and short reach, quarks glued, binding energy; the weak force: which nuclei are stable and how the rest decay), and how to change them; How it works, the readme and the build guide say the same.
433. "its hitting like 9fps zooming into a planet on planet fall liek its still trying to simulate the sky when its not even in the view port". Found in the code: falling to a world, everything behind it was still drawn every frame (the web's million and a half points, every galaxy's stars, their clusters and nebulae, the stars round you), and the stars round you were put on the card again every frame though nothing about them changed. Now when the world fills the whole view nothing behind it is drawn (what is in front, its moons and rings, still is), and the stars round you go to the card only when they change.
434. "and it pauses at 500 nanometer get rid of pauses like that it makes it look prozen", "and from 500 -1 nano meters its just static appearance", "then atoms pop in all at once in eroor", "and looks like handoff in the 500-1 nano range". Found in the code: in the matter the tour's zoom stopped dead, for up to four seconds, until the view was over what was picked; the grain's face was the same roughness at every size (the atomic terraces its notes promised were never drawn on grains), so from 500 nm down the zoom looked still; and the crystal's atoms came in only between 10 and 5 nm, onto a face with no atoms in it. Now the zoom slows to a quarter while it lines up and never stops; below about a micrometre the face steps in single atomic layers (0.3 nm) into terraces with a fine line at each step, growing flatter and opening out as you come in; and from about 200 nm the face shows its atoms, rows 0.25 nm apart, sharpening as you come in, so the atoms made in full arrive on a face that already shows them. All on the card.
435. "look what happens on zoom in to a ice giant im sduure this is nt the only problem one". Seen on the screen: inside an ice giant's molecular hydrogen layer, its cloud deck drawn from underneath, the seams between its pieces a grid across the view and its storms blown up huge. Found in the code: every giant's shell was drawn the same from inside as from out. Now below any giant's cloud tops (gas giants, ice giants, hot Jupiters: every world with no ground) the shell is not drawn; the view is the giant's own gas all round, in its air's colour, dimming as you go deeper and lit on its day side, and nothing past it (the sky) is drawn.
436. "it does a weird thing were glaxies webbing isnt viewable until zoom out then it persists on zoom in which is fine i suppsoe i just needs to be uniform". Found in the code: the web's light (up to 900,000 points, each found by sampling the web's foam, up to sixty tries a point) was made on one low-priority thread while every other core built the world, so at the start it could take a long while to show; once made it stayed, so after a trip out it was always there. Now it is made on every core but one at once, each its own share from its own seed (the same points every time), so it shows a few seconds after the start, the same on every visit.
437. "lets put a loading bar or somehting on start till everything is properly ready or what? its a free wheel loading problem". The start now holds on a loading screen (the dark of space, a bar, and what is being made: the cosmic web's light by the share made, the galaxies made in full round you by how many have their stars) until what is seen first is ready; the controls and the tour wait, a filmed tour does not film it, and it never holds more than a minute. Then the universe opens whole, never filling in in front of you.
438. "and planets spped is way to fast or time scale is way to fast the star is rising like every second". Found in the code: time began at an hour a second, so on a world with a 10-hour day its star rose every ten seconds and raced across the sky, and close moons went round in seconds. It now begins at a minute a second: a day of 10 to 40 hours passes in 10 to 40 minutes, the worlds and their stars moving calmly; faster with . or the Time menu.
439. "and when it looks up at the sky it pans too much and with the fasst time speen it makes me dizzy". Found in the tour: on the ground it tilted up 55 degrees in under five seconds while turning, then circled the sky at 12 degrees a second (up to 15 with the director's choice) while the sky itself turned with the world's spin, then dropped 85 degrees in four. Now it tilts up over eight seconds turning barely (1.5 degrees a second), circles the sky at 3, and looks down over seven; with time starting at a minute a second (438) the sky itself barely turns.
440. "it froze an unknow exemtioon error at matter delpht where teh static was before". Found: the graphics driver timed out (Windows' event log: nvlddmkm 153 at 23:48:13, and twelve more times earlier the same day, before any of the night's changes) on the first frame of a grain's face, 103 micrometres above a hematite grain; the frame took longer than the two seconds Windows allows, so it reset the card and the next present failed (0x887A0005). Found in the code: a point of light had no upper size unless its draw asked for one, so close in, soft glows (gas, molecules, the matter's glows) each covered the whole screen, thousands deep. Now no point ever covers more than most of the view, and none behind you is drawn; the crash note says plainly when the card stopped answering.
441. "asnd wehy does it say dna log when we are working on the universe" / "oh we are doing both". The zoom and path logs were named for DNA-AND from before the universe had its own program; each program now writes its own (universe_zoom.log, dnaand_zoom.log).
442. "make sure nothing else is labels wrong between projects". Audited both. In the shared code every name a person can see now comes from the program's own name (`PreviewText.AppName`): thread names, the window and document-window classes, the save-file check, the sample and view titles; settings and logs were already each its own. The Universe project's own copy carries its own name throughout: universe-sync.cmd now runs installer/universe-names.ps1 on it, so its code's namespace is Universe (not DnaAnd), its file headers and content files' labels say Universe, its icon maker draws only its own spiral galaxy, and DNA-AND's helix icon and the sync's own script are left out; the About page no longer sends players to DNA-AND. What still says DNA in the Universe project is real science (DNA molecules, the Helix Nebula).
443. "Universe gets its own (Recommended)" (asked: should Universe's content files get their own extension, not .dnat, DNA-AND text). The Universe project's content files are .unt (Universe text): the sync renames every content file and every mention in its code, content and documents (the loader reads *.unt there); DNA-AND keeps .dnat.
444. "make sure all docs are good to go" / "in game and out" / "the readme last i saw was pretty shit". Every Universe document checked against the code, in the game and out: no longer "without a single loading screen" (there is the one at the start; none along the way); no fixed tour length (its sky shots are slower now); a body's pull and escape speed are on the cards of what has a known mass, not every card; in the game, a click, the wheel or a key pauses the tour (not T to fly it yourself); start-up options, the landing page's buttons and the links between the documents all match. The readme on the project's web page is still the old one until the new one is pushed.
445. "fix the files of the docs fully and completely to how ive already descirbbeed many many times then do full cascade for both projects all four remotes" / "and not a emohji desacster like dna and for the docs". Built to the creator's own words for docs (no text walls, easy and fun to read, beautifully written and laid out, tooltips, the game's own images, links, the download links at the top, as if complete), without emoji clutter: the Universe readme opens with the game's own galaxy icon (drawn by its own code) over a centred title, both download links (Unity AI Lab Git and GitHub) with tooltips, a line of facts and links to every guide, then the recording of one zoom from the galaxies to the ground, and short sections of tables; the guides and credits open with the icon and the same tooltipped links between all the documents; every emoji is gone from every Universe document, in the game and out. The in-game viewer reads the documents' bits of web page (title, bold, links, line breaks), leaves out pictures and their captions, link tooltips and empty table headings.
446. "and make sure the readme properly describnbes how we make such a massive universe with only 1.7 Mb or what ever the count is now". Measured: the installer 0.59 MB, the program 0.86 MB, every template 0.26 MB, everything installed 1.7 MB. The readme now says so up top and, in "A whole universe in under 2 MB", how: a seed, not a save; templates, not copies; equations, not pictures; only what you can see; the graphics card doing the work; the 1.7 MB the recipe, not the meal. The About page in the game says it briefly.
447. "the comet was a ghost and the pan from astroid to commet was jerk as fuck". Seen in the tour's own clip, frame by frame: on picking the comet the view whipped most of the way round (the turn to a new subject took at most three and a half seconds however far, up to about seventy degrees a second), and closing on the comet its coma's points stacked into a white blaze over the whole view, the nucleus barely seen. Now a turn to a new subject takes as long as it needs at no more than about twenty degrees a second, eased in and out, the shot lengthened to hold it; and a comet's coma is drawn first and thins as you come in (bright from afar, a faint haze inside it, as its gas really is), its dark nucleus drawn over it.
448. "and tiadally loceked jsut means a moon or something is always near one side of the planet or body due to orboitoitalk distance of like moons and stuff". Tidal locking is not a kind of world: any world close enough to its star turns once each orbit, one face always toward it (a law: tidal-lock in content/physics.dnat, about half an AU for a star of one solar mass, growing with the cube root of its mass). Its day is its year, its card says so, and its day side is scorched and night side frozen whatever its kind; moons already turn once each orbit round their planet. A seen tidally locked world's blue river lines (a dry world drawing rivers in water's colour) are now dry beds.
449. "and some planets ave straihgt line surface coloring and pattrering< nature sdoesnt build in straight lines". Seen on screen: clouds in diamond and hexagon patches. Found in the code: the cloud cover was worked out only at the mesh's corners with a sharp edge, so cloud edges ran along its triangles; and every layer of the ground's and clouds' noise stood on the same square grid. Now a world's cloud cover is worked out for every pixel on the card, and every finer layer of noise, on the card and on the processor, is turned to its own angle, so nothing lines up in rows.
450. "it froze an unknow exemtioon error" again (the driver timing out at 00:11:56, about 180 micrometres over a tidally locked world, in the band where the ground's sand is worked out grain by grain for every pixel) and "200 million for a micron sounds high". Each pixel searched 27 cells (two layers each) for the grain on top of it, some 400 million checks a frame on a 3840 x 2024 screen; a grain is at most half a cell across with its middle in the middle of its cell, so the one on top of a point is in the eight nearest cells: now eight, under a third of the work. "at 20 microns im seeing space and only white and black texture" is still to see on screen. The run's clip was never finished (the game closed by the crash before it could be), which is why it would not open.
451. "and remmeber planets can be moons and moons planets and all these bodies orbits are too perfect reall orbits are eliptical and wild in cvariation". Every orbit is now an ellipse (a law: orbits in content/physics.dnat): most planets gently oval (a Rayleigh spread of 0.12, as surveys find), a share stretched into long ellipses (0.3 to 0.85, as many round other stars are), close moons near round, far captured ones stretched; each body moves by Kepler's equation, fastest at its closest point, and its card says how oval its orbit is and its closest and farthest. Moons are worlds too: each chooses its kind from the same templates as the planets by its moon-weight and where its planet circles the star (new templates for airless rocky and icy moons, and any planet kind can be a moon now and then: ocean, lava, temperate, rocky, ice); nothing in the code names a moon any more (its grains, air and life come from its template).
452. "it pauses at 10570 light years way to long when it needs to look around a little instead of still frame pause for 10 seconds". Found in the code: a tour's pick that finds nothing yet (the black hole at a galaxy's heart still being made round you, or a small galaxy with none to find) waited up to twenty seconds without moving. Now while any pick looks it never sits still: it looks round slowly, eased in, and goes on after five seconds at most.
453. "zopomed to a comet and nothign was there and wtf u should be able to see comets all the way across a solar system". Found in the code: a comet's coma reached only about 10,000 km and its tails about 300,000 km (two thousand and sixty thousand times a 5 km nucleus), so from across its system it was a speck, and the tour flew to what looked like nothing. Now its coma and tail are their real sizes as traits of the kind (content/bodies.dnat coma-km 100,000 to 1,000,000, tail-km 10 to 80 million), each comet its own in the range and its card saying so; the coma and tails are drawn each to its own size, and a comet is drawn wherever its tail spans a few pixels, so it shows across its whole system.
454. "and we should default day only cycles on". Always day (Time menu) now starts on: every world in daylight unless it is turned off for true day and night; the readme and the in-game readme say so.
455. "i tried to f12 at 12 microns but it crashed". The driver timed out again (00:30:26 and 00:30:28) at 106 micrometres over a rocky planet: the very height where the ground hands you on into the matter (a third of a grain), the first frame of a grain's face, where it had timed out every time that day. Found in the code: the grain's face worked out its detail on the card for every pixel octave by octave, as many as the pixel's size asked, up to forty, each past the stored twelve running mode-E (up to twelve Newton steps and four cell lookups, three times over); twenty-eight of those on every pixel of a big screen were seconds of work in one frame. Now no pixel ever works out more than fourteen octaves (the twelve stored, two of mode-E); finer than that the face's detail is its atomic terraces and atoms, and then the atoms made in full. A clip being filmed is now finished if the program crashes, so what was filmed still plays; screenshots, clips and the landing page's window are named for their own program.
456. "go ahead and clear all the old clips" / "and the installer should have option to delete all the old clips on new install/update". The five clips in the installed copy's captures folder deleted (screenshots kept). Then "no it need delet options for all of them" / "not just clips": installing over an earlier copy, the installer offers on its Shortcuts page to delete each thing that copy left behind, each on its own and saying how many files and how big: its clips (ticked to start with), its screenshots and its settings (back to how it starts; both unticked to start with).
457. "the comet is transdpartent i can see stars through it" / "i mean asstroid but comet prolly same problem". Found in the code: the solid small bodies (asteroids, comets' nuclei) were drawn before the stars round you, the star clusters' stars, the belts and the orbit lines, and those glows are drawn without looking at what is in front, so they showed over a solid rock. Now the solid small bodies and worlds' rings are drawn last of everything in space, after every star, belt and orbit behind them. (A comet's coma and tails stay see-through, as real ones are.)
458. "also im clicking around at 1km scale and i cant select anything". Seen in the game's own window: over a temperate world at 3 km, the title bar said "going to a spot on Temperate world": the click had picked, but the cards and the scale were hidden (H, which any key during the tour also reaches), so nothing showed it. Now a click that picks anything (in space, on the ground, in the matter) brings the cards back.
459. "and im just sitting at 1km and its shittly doing 14fps im not even zooming or nothing". Measured on the running game: the card 56% busy at 15 frames a second, the main thread busy about 40% of the time, the rest of its time waiting: each frame was handed to the card and waited for in full before the next frame's game work began, so the processor and the card took turns. Now a frame is handed to the card and waited for only when the next frame's commands are recorded, so the next frame's game work (the world, the tour, the simulation) runs while the card draws (a screenshot or a clip's frame still waits, to read the picture back; freed buffers were already held back eight frames).
460. "comet didnt have a auros or gas or a tail facing away from star and i could nt see it at solar systems scale like comets should be seen" / "it looks just like an astroid". Found in the code: the cap on how big a point may be (440) was set in the points' own units, so wherever points are drawn scaled to their real size (a comet's coma and tails, black holes' discs and jets, a star's surface and corona, worlds' rings, galaxies) it shrank with the scale: a comet's coma and tails to nothing, leaving only its nucleus; and the comet's smallest point size had lost its scale too (453). Now the cap is measured on the screen itself (no point spreads more than about a third of the screen from its middle), whatever the scale, and a comet's coma and tails keep their smallest size as a share of the view's distance, so a comet shows its coma and its tails straight away from its star across its whole system.
461. "at 200 mm ant micor level it crashes still" (the driver timing out again at 00:55:06, about 185 micrometres over an ice world, the ground's hand-over to the matter, with every shader loop bounded, the octaves capped and the palette in range). A reset of the driver means one command never finished (a loop that never ends, or a read the card cannot make), not a slow frame, so the game now keeps Windows' own record of what the card does (DRED: each command as it is finished, and the address of a read it could not make), names every draw by what it draws (the web's light, galaxies, the stars round you, the star system, asteroids and comets, a world's ground with its sand, its clouds, its sea, the grains over the ground, a grain's face), notes every buffer, and when the card is lost writes to the crash log why, which draw it died on with its neighbours, and which buffer a bad read fell in.
462. "200m ther are lock hand offs" / "mm". Found in the code: the camera is held 0.6 of a ground piece above the finest piece built under it, and finer pieces were asked for only once the camera came near them, so on the way down it stopped at each level, waited for the four finer pieces, dropped, and stopped again. Now the finer pieces under the camera are asked for four levels ahead of it (all four of each level, as a piece descends only when all four are made), built by the workers before the camera gets there, so the floor never holds it waiting. Also found while reading: the near stones passed 1 in the slot that now carries an atomic layer's height (455), so every stone got terraces a stone-radius high and atom dots the size of the stone; a stone passes 0 (none).
463. Found while reading the screenshot path ("i tried to f12 at 12 microns but it crashed", the F12 picture of the towers never saved): with a tour being filmed, F12 grabs the next frame at full size for the picture, and the recorder was handed that full-size frame too, which it cannot take, so it threw and the recording was thrown away (its error overwritten in the log by later lines, until 461). Now a frame that is not the clip's size is skipped and the clip goes on. The `.claude/` guard hook gains a per-project opt-out (the marker `.claude/.claude-public-ok`, written only by the owner's decision): these two projects are public with `.claude/` in them by the creator's word, and the guard had begun blocking every push.
464. "astroids and comets and shit dont have stellar ids". Every body round you now carries an id on its card as the stars and worlds do: a code for its kind (BH a black hole, NS a neutron star or pulsar, RP a rogue planet, AS an asteroid, KO a Kuiper belt object, CM a comet, IS a star between galaxies) and six digits from where it is, the same body the same id every time.
465. "the look up to thew sky at human ground level inproprly says its star and other worlds when worlds are viewable and  no star is in the sky at the moment". The sky stop's caption was fixed words in the tour's script. Now a caption of {sky} is written on the spot from what is really up from where you stand: its star only if it is above the horizon and in view, this world's moons and the star's other worlds only if they are, the galaxy's stars by night, the day sky by day.
466. "at its hitting 5fps at 5mm pan and it should not pan at those zoom levels only when at atom level and there is shit to see besides gravel". The turning at the gravel was the slow look-round a waiting pick makes (452), which belongs out in space: by a world, among its stones and grains, a waiting pick now holds still. The frames: every stone and grain drawn over the ground had its stains and cracks worked out per pixel in double precision (most cards run doubles many times slower than floats), and under them the ground still worked out its own sand per pixel though the grains covered it. The stains and cracks are now single precision (colour needs no more; the height stays double), and below a centimetre, once the real grains are drawn, the ground's per-pixel sand is off.
467. "when i click on the app it doesnt propelry bring it to top focus it starys behind other windows till i close them". Nothing in the window refused activation and the played window is not made "never activate" (only timed checks are), so it no longer relies on Windows' default: a click anywhere in the game's window brings it to the front and gives it the keyboard, and Windows' question on a click ("may I activate you?") is answered yes outright.
468. "are obits suppsoe to be in a flat plane thats not normal is it" / "no fix it dont leav e it". Worlds born in one flat disc do lie in nearly one plane (ours within a few degrees, Mercury 7, Pluto 17; round other stars mostly 1 to 3 apart), and the game had that, but systems stirred by a passing star or a second star have their worlds scattered tens of degrees apart (Upsilon Andromedae's two outer planets 30 degrees). Now the spread is a trait of the system kind (content/solar-systems.dnat tilt-spread, wild-tilt-share, wild-tilt), and a new kind, the stirred system, forms by its share (12 in 100, chosen from the seed itself so every other system stays as it was) with its worlds 12 degrees apart on average and three in ten 20 to 45 degrees out.
469. "all type of compbinations even ultrrqa fast bodies willth stained long elipticals theh phsysics should determine it" / "relativity is propelty in , or no?". Orbits now follow from speeds, as the laws of motion have it: each body moves at some share of the circular speed where it was placed (content/physics.dnat orbits: planet-speed-spread, wild-share and wild-speed, moon-close-speed and moon-far-speed; a share flung near escape speed into long thin loops flown ultra-fast through their closest point), and its ellipse, period and speed everywhere follow (vis-viva); the cards give the speed now, at its closest and at its farthest. Relativity: it was in for a black hole's horizon (Schwarzschild) and the light it bends (an Einstein ring), not for orbits or time; now every orbit's closest point turns round its star by 6 pi G M / (c^2 a (1 - e^2)) an orbit (Mercury's 43 arcseconds a century; whole turns near a black hole), and the cards say how much slower clocks run there by the star's or the black hole's pull. Length contraction, redshift and frame dragging are still to come.
470. "at 500 pico the atmoms jsut pop into view incorrectly" / "and i dont want a fad in". Found in the code: each atom of a crystal was two drawings cross-faded between 1 nanometre and 500 picometres (one set of electrons fading out as another faded in), and the lattice itself faded in from 30 to 5 nanometres. Now there is no fading: an atom is there at its real size and is drawn once it spans about a pixel (the face's own dots, worked out per pixel on the card, stand for the ones too small to draw), a bond once it spans a few; one drawing of each atom at every size.
471. "and it generic says a proton of a nuclues incorrectly saying which element it is". A proton's or neutron's card now says whose nucleus it is (a nucleus of oxygen: 8 protons, 8 neutrons), and a quark's which nucleon of which element.
472. "ii see grains then it instandt swticheds to balck and white elctron view of material which is fine(might ass note color doesnt exist at this scalle and later when ther is color properly do it" / "not black and white elctron view but like elecrton microscopre vioew where everything turns black and white before the color of electorns and stuff appear". Colour is light of a wavelength (about 400 to 700 nanometres), so nothing smaller can be seen in colour: now as the view narrows from 700 to 400 nanometres across, the grain's face and its stones go to greys, as an electron microscope sees them (on the card, with the mineral's colour above), and the atoms and electrons, drawn in their marked colours, bring colour back as they come into view; the card says so. A true rendering of light this small is for later.
473. "and with fps i want gpu and cpu stats also togfgle able in settings". With the frames a second (top right) the stats now, when ticked (the landing page's Show GPU and CPU stats, and View > Show GPU and CPU stats; the setting show-stats, on to start with): the frame's time; the processor's time on the frame (from its start to handing it to the card) and this program's share of every core (GetProcessTimes) and its memory (GetProcessMemoryInfo); the card's time on the frame (the timestamps), how busy it is, and the card's own memory this program holds of what Windows allows it (IDXGIAdapter3::QueryVideoMemoryInfo, the adapter kept for it); the card's name; the detail and the ground pieces waiting. Sampled twice a second (src/platform/Stats.cs), so they cost nothing; the card sized to its longest line, flush right.
474. "we need a full directory listing of the docs and their make uup and use descriptions in the doc virewer that well formated easy to read and use and beautiful". A directory of every document, first in the viewer's list and first in the Help menu (Documents: what's here; the Guide and wiki button opens it): which to read for what; for anyone, for builders, for coders, and the credits, each with what it is made of and how long it takes; where they live (in the program, in the project, the licence); and how to use the viewer (the list, Find across every page, Back, the text size, links). docs/release/IN-GAME-DOCS.md, built in as docs.md; in Universe docs/in-game/DOCS.md.
475. "the docs are all properly formated and created and designed around explaining the systems and shit technical and non techinical dos where basic readmes and how tos are laymens and technical docs are more involved" / "this is a tool menat to be stolen and used for any number of projects of any types wehere a universe is needed". The split made plain and kept: the readme, the in-game About, Readme and How it works in plain words for anyone; Build your own universe for builders with no code; Build your own (technical) for coders; the readme's Which guide table says who each is for, and the documents directory (474) too. The readme has a Take it section (MIT; a game, a simulation, a film or screensaver, a classroom, or a piece of it), the guide's and the technical guide's intros and licence say it is built to be taken, and the About and the zip's readme say so. The readme's stray table fragment under the 2 MB section is gone.
476. "and readmes need to propelryy talk of how the app is totaly scatch made from the compiler to the engine explaine it all in laymens terms and tefchnical respective to the doc". Told straight in each doc at its level: the readme's Built from nothing (no engine, no libraries, no art, no build tools, our own formats, and exactly what it borrows from Windows: Direct3D 12 and its shader compiler, Media Foundation for the MP4, the framework's Deflate in a PNG; the compiler is the one inside Windows, not ours); How it works' Built from scratch, a paragraph for the curious; the technical guide's Everything above the compiler is ours, a layer-by-layer table of what is ours and what is Windows', with the counts (about 18,000 lines of C# and shader in 61 files, 10,000 lines of templates in 30); the About and the zip's readme in a line.
477. "the full universe in 2mb and less is a major achievement.. is it not? no thing else does this does it dont be pushy beut be real and do checks on reall shit that does similar with comparison tables of like top universe sims of stats and size and comput issues or out preformance and such". Checked against each project's own published numbers and put in the readme's Against the field table with its sources (Noctis IV 1 to 5 MB for one galaxy of 78 billion stars; Elite 22 KB for 2,048 planets; .kkrieger 96 KB for a shooter level; SpaceEngine 4 GB, 50 GB with HD packs; Elite Dangerous about 25 GB for 400 billion systems; No Man's Sky 6 GB at launch, 10 to 15 GB now, for 18 quintillion planets; Universe Sandbox 4 GB; Celestia 1.6 about 36 MB of catalogues; Pioneer about 550 MB), and said plainly: nothing in the table goes from the cosmic web to a quark in one camera, and nothing but the two tiny pioneers comes near the size; and, to be fair, the others carry sound, art, gameplay and years of polish, this is a 0.1 preview without life yet, and it needs a Direct3D 12 card because the pixels are worked out there. A short version in the About.
478. "and it better be rela infinate zoom in the code so if its not fix it" / "so docs are accurate". It was not: the zoom in stopped at a hundredth of a femtometre (MatterView.BottomKm = 1e-20 km, clamped in UniverseView). Now there is no floor: the point you ride (a quark, or an electron) is kept exactly at the focus once you are all but at it (a rounding's width is a whole screen far enough in), its glow fills the view and stays, the scale bar names attometres, zeptometres, yoctometres, rontometres and quectometres and then the number itself, and the card says what is known there: inside a quark past anything measured (a quark shows no size down to 1e-19 m), and below the Planck length (1.6e-35 m) not even length. BottomKm is now 1e-290 km, only where double precision gives out, 10^255 times below the Planck length. Outward you are held inside the visible universe (HoldInside), because that is all there is to see; the docs say both.
479. "and add a fly by a balck hole thats feeding and zoom into it on flyby to see its acression disk of a eating balckhole and zoome to the gravity distortions and points of interest" / "before planet level zoom in". The tour's far-galaxy pick now prefers a galaxy whose heart is feeding (worked out from the same place-hash the hole itself uses, with its kind's feeding share from content/bodies.dnat), and the black-hole pick a feeding one before a quiet one. After flying down to it, three new shots before the star and the worlds: round its accretion disc from above (10 radii, rising 40 degrees), edge-on at 4 radii from below so the light of the disc's far side and the stars behind bend over it, and in to 1.3 radii at the shadow, then back out to 200 radii under a caption written from the hole itself ({hole}: its jets if it fires them, its disc, or quiet; its horizon's size and mass). The hole's shadow is 2.6 horizon radii, the shots' radii are in that unit.
480. "and the repo urls need properl social images and write ups and" / "can that be done on github and git?". Both, nearly all of it by their APIs: each repo's description (the write-up), website and topics are set on GitHub (gh repo edit) and on the lab's Forgejo (PATCH the repo, PUT its topics), and the Forgejo repo's avatar (its share image for links) is uploaded by its API from docs/img/avatar.png. The link-preview image itself (docs/img/social.png, 1280 by 640: the logo, the name, the tagline, the facts, over a frame of the journey clip) is made by our own script from the game's own pictures; GitHub has no API for its social preview, so that one file is uploaded once by hand in the repo's Settings, Social preview. Universe's are in docs/img of its own repo, DNA-AND's in docs/img here.
481. "look at the open text viewer there is text under the highleighted text that i can scroll to and is a dispaly issue". Seen on screen: the page's lines ran off the right edge with no wrap, and the list clipped its headings. The cause: the page had its own margins (EM_SETRECT) and the box's own zoom for the text size (EM_SETZOOM, A+ and A-); with both, the box laid the lines out at the unzoomed width and drew them bigger, so at any size above 100% every paragraph overflowed by the zoom (the tables were fitted by hand with 100/zoom, which is why only they fit). Now the text size is in the formatting itself: every size in the rich text (type, line spacing, space before and after, indents, cell padding) is scaled by the zoom before the page is laid out, so it is always laid out at its true width; the page is laid out again at the same place (by the character its first line starts at) on a zoom or a resize, and Back returns by character too. The list is wider (340 px, or 30% of the window) with a shorter heading indent.
482. "took a shot image stars ar only populating in half the screen or its a shading thing not allowing them to materialize". Seen in the shot: a galaxy's real stars dense on one side of the view, thin on the other, with a soft cut across. The cause: the stars were made in a fixed 3 by 3 by 3 block of 25-light-year cubes round the camera's cube, not round the camera, so near one face of its cube there were about 26 light-years of made stars one way and 49 the other. Now every cube whose centre is within a reach of where you are is made (real-stars-cube-ly 25 and real-stars-reach-ly 60 in content/universe.dnat), the made ones kept while in reach so a move makes only the new face, the sky as deep in stars on every side; the fast-crossing rule as before.
483. "and it like zooms in and out of the same galazxy when it should fly like 1-5 billion light years to a whole nother galaxy on web galaxy view zoom out" / "its like it keeps selecting the closest". It could only choose among the galaxies already made round you (within near-ly, eight million light-years), so it was always a neighbour. Now the far-galaxy pick takes a crossing from the stop (far-ly: 1e9..5e9 in the tour): a destination that far ahead of you, inside the visible universe; the regions round it hold the same galaxies they always will (each from its own place), so they are made then and there (SpawnRegion) and held until you arrive (the whole region, DropFarGalaxies), one whose heart is feeding first and another kind than the last; and while crossing faster than a couple of regions a frame nothing is made on the way (the web's glow stands for it), so thousands of galaxies' stars are never asked for at once. The caption says so.
484. "and dont zoom in to water worlds as it not propelry done yet as the fall to planet is under water or at least it looks like it is a 500mm level" / "need to fix water worlds and water zommming in to go to bed rock" / "i took a scrren shot of that issue" / "if we can get all the worlds working correctly i dont care what it zooms into". The shot: the descent went into the sea at the 500-micrometre level, among sea-water molecules drawn as grains. Now the dive is built: over water the surface is the floor until you are a metre from it, then the sea bed is (UniverseView.Surface, _underSea), and you go on down through the water: a fill and fog in the water's colour with the star's light dimmed by depth (gone within a few hundred metres; PlanetTerrain.Dim), everything beyond about thirty metres lost in it, the bed drawn through it and refined to its grains like land. At the bed the matter is the sea floor's own mix (content/minerals.dnat @mix ocean: silt, sand, augite, anorthite, calcite, clay) with water molecules above the grains packed as a liquid is (SetAirAbove with the liquid's density), never the sea-water-as-grains of before. The tour, besides, comes down on dry land (a land pick: the nearest dry spot under you, rings of directions outward) and never picks a world that is all sea (land-share at or below zero with water); the symptoms the sea path gave ("and qwerks never loaded and it says a spot in the seas on 200 attometers"; "and just beforee atom view its a weird grid like checkbaord of differnt colors so the elctron microscope vioew neever happend on the water world") came from that path and go with it: at the bed the grain face, its greys and its nuclei are the same as on land.
485. "and eatoms are all wobbel;y looks nosiating the atom is wobbleing even when focused on". A liquid's molecules were drawn swaying with time (its thermal motion, slowed), so the atom you were on wobbled with the view. No sway now: an atom's thermal motion is ten trillion times a second, which no eye could see as movement, only as the blur its electron cloud already is; a liquid's sites stay shaken off their places, still.
486. "ie i should be able to find a gold astroid or nickle being most common ect ect and zoom infinated on any body or abject" / "stars shoiuld be mostly hydrogen till getting to it heavey m,etal cores ect ect". On the roadmap, in those words: every body a place to land and zoom into without end, with its real make-up from a template (asteroids by class: stony, carbonaceous, metal of iron and nickel with gold and platinum in their real traces; comets' ice and dust; stars mostly hydrogen and helium down through their layers to the heavier elements at the core), the way worlds already are.
487. "make notes in document this prokject code is mostly not human readible, right? thats correct right?". Measured before answering, and the answer is no, not as said: it is dense, not unreadable. 15,406 code lines in 61 files; the middle line 57 characters; a fifth of all lines plain English (1,866 comment lines and 1,179 trailing ones), every block saying what it does and why; but one line in ten carries three or more statements, one in fourteen runs past 160 characters (the longest 1,058), names are short, and the big files are long (UniverseView 3,122 lines, MatterView 1,888, PlanetTerrain 1,550). Written into the technical guide as a Reading the code section (what it is like, how to read it by its comments, where it is densest and easiest, the rule it follows), and a line in the builders' guide and the documents directory; "mostly not human readable" was not written, because it is not true.
488. "what i mean is without AI only advance coders can manually edit it". Yes, and that is the right way to say it: written into the technical guide's Reading the code as Who can change what (the templates, anyone with a text editor; the code by hand, an advanced programmer at home in C#, Direct3D 12 at the COM level, HLSL and double-precision numerics, a careless edit in the hot paths breaking the picture; with an AI coding assistant, anyone, the comments being written so that one can read a block, understand it and change it safely), and in the builders' guide, the readme's Which guide table and the documents directory.
489. "its not who can do what its a suggestion". Reworded as one, everywhere it was said (the technical guide's row is now A suggestion; the builders' guide, the readme's Which guide table and the documents directory say "we suggest"): change the templates freely; for the code, unless you are an advanced programmer, we suggest an AI coding assistant rather than editing by hand, the comments being written for that. No gate on anyone.
490. "at 1 aU scale the stars are appearing in grids and somene ggrids no starts like squares of starless areas i took a screenshots". The shots: the stars on a lattice, with the gaps between its planes as empty squares. Float precision, not the cubes: the real stars were kept in the galaxy's own frame (a star 0.8 galaxy units out has float steps of about 190 AU) and the camera was subtracted from them on the card in float, so at AU scale every star snapped to a 190-AU grid. Now each star is an offset from the cube the list was built round (exact to a fraction of an AU), the cached cubes hold their stars within the cube, and the list's origin is placed in the universe and the camera taken from it in double before the card sees it; the nearest-star search, star positions and picks use the same origin.
491. "and its like zooming around with no focus". The same cause: the star the tour or a click flies to was placed from the snapped position, so the view circled a point that was not quite the star. Fixed with 490.
492. "and another thing on zoom out u see the stars all like converge in a white fuzz pattern as zooming out fast that looks scetch". The real stars round you (thousands within 60 light-years, each a dot never smaller than a few pixels) were drawn at every view, so a view a thousand light-years wide still carried that block of dots round the eye, a fuzz of light. Now they fade out once the view is wider than about three times the reach they are made in and are gone by fifteen times it, where the galaxy's own star field stands for them. If a fuzz remains on a fast zoom out, a screenshot of it will say what it is.
493. "A bar shows the universe being made, then it opens, whole. there is no loading bar". It was there and gone: on a fast machine the web's light and the galaxies round you are made in well under a second, so the bar was on screen for a few frames. Now the loading screen is held a moment and a half at the least (its bar still the true share), so it is seen; never longer than the making takes beyond that.
494. "right at 50 mico meters there is an instant hand off change over and i took a bunch of shots each with its own obvious viewable problems". Six shots on the way out of an ice grain, each its own fault, each found in the code and fixed. The hand-over: the planet's ground was not drawn at all once the matter view began (at three tenths of the grain's size: 300 micrometres on a millimetre ice grain), so the bed and the grains round you vanished at a cut and a flat floor at the focus grain's top took their place. Now the ground is drawn behind the matter at every size (the matter drawn over it with the depth cleared, as before), and the matter's own level floor is used only where there is no ground (the gas of space); where it is, it lies at the bed the grain rests on, never at the grain's top.
495. The grey pillars standing off the face at 1.7 micrometres (the towers, spikes and lines reported before) and the hatch of straight lines across it at 83 micrometres: the face's rings of points are worked out a budget a frame and only in view, and a point not yet worked out (or out of view) stood at height zero; the easing of each finer ring toward the coarser one averaged a real point with those zeros and pulled it up to the focus top, a wall at every other point along the edge of what was made: a fence of pillars from the side, a grid of lines from above. Now a point eases only among neighbours that are worked out, its slope and hollow are taken only from them, and three times as many points are worked out a frame, so the edge of what is made is a plain edge (the ground behind it now) and never a wall.
496. The per-pixel detail's finest layer came in at the pixel's own size (a wavelength of two pixels at full weight), which is moire: with every layer turned to its own angle, straight lines in rows in several directions. Now no layer is finer than four pixels across and the finest is eased in over a couple of pixels' worth, on the face and on everything the card details.
497. The dark stair-stepped skirts under the grains at half a millimetre and a millimetre: below a centimetre the grains are the ground's own pieces raised under each to its top (the matter lays nothing over the ground), and a piece's triangles shade flat, so a dome's sides stepped. The per-pixel grains (each pixel's grain found again on the card, with its true rounded normal) were switched off below a centimetre because, looked up from the raised dome, a pixel fell into the cell above and found another grain, a doubled bed. Now the lookup is flattened to the bed's level (the shader already carries it), every pixel of a dome finds its own grain, and the per-pixel grains stay on at every height: the domes shade smooth, the colours stay one bed.
498. The dotted outlines on pale ice at 24 millimetres, a sketch of rings: the gap between grains was drawn at a quarter of the ground's brightness with a hard edge, so on a bed of pale grains the only thing seen was a ring of dark dots round each (the hard edge aliased to dots). Now a grain's edge is eased over a pixel and the gap is half the brightness, in shadow but not black.
499. "and when it pans from astroid to comet the astroid just instanat disappears intstead of smoth pan and zoom away". A shot glides in the log of its distance from its new subject: from ninety kilometres off an asteroid, the first frame of a shot to a comet a billion and a half kilometres away moved the camera two hundred thousand kilometres, and the asteroid was gone in one frame. Now a shot's departure is paced from what was in view where it began: the camera is never farther from that spot than max-rate tenfolds a second allows, from that size (found each frame along the shot's own path), so the asteroid pulls away and shrinks smoothly, the look turning as before; once the shot's own glide is within the allowance it goes on as written, its arrival eased as before, and the shot is not over until the camera has arrived. Every shot gets this: nothing you were beside is left in a frame.
500. "at atom scale the elctrons astart moving way to fast and i dont see their orbital paths that are constantly changing like electrons do". The electrons go round at a third of the speed they did (slowed to be watched: the inner, more tightly held ones still faster than the outer, as the charge they feel has it), and every path is seen: 48 marks along it in the orbital's own shape (s round, p through its two lobes, d round its leaves, f its six), brighter than before, and the whole set of paths turns on slowly with time, each subshell and orbital its own way (a precession, a whole turn in about two minutes), with the electron on it, so no path is ever the same twice.
501. "and when looking at a galxy form distance but not too far i cant tell the differnce betwen startts and glaxies in the background webbing idk how we would asddress that maybe tone down one and brighten the other correctly at appropriate distances and scales". Done the way suggested: the web's points (each a far galaxy, a soft glow) are dimmed to a quarter and drawn at half their size as you come within a galaxy's reach, by how far in you are, while the galaxy's own stars stay sharp points in their own colours; from a galaxy's edge the two are never taken for each other, and out among the web the points are as they were.
502. In the matter the ground is not being made, but its old count of pieces waiting (650 in the shots) still made the tour say it was making what is ahead and slowed its zoom; in the matter that count now means nothing to the tour. And deep in, past the face's rings, the matter's backdrop is just under the face again (never black past the rings), while above a tenth of a millimetre the planet's ground is the backdrop.
503. "and where is the performance and comparison table of other sims and space games and space simulations" / "it need proper naming and listing in the doc lists not burried oin other docs". It was a section of the readme and a short table in the About. Now it is its own document, Against the field: size, scope, performance (docs/release/UNIVERSE-COMPARED.md; docs/COMPARED.md in Universe; built in as compared.md): the size-and-scope table with its sources, a new table of what each program needs (its own published minimum: card, memory, disk) and how Universe runs (any Direct3D 12 card; the frame rates on the lab's machine from the stats on screen: 60 among the galaxies and at a star, 45 to 60 among the atoms, 19 to 26 on the grains while the ground is made), where it stands, and what is fair to say for the others. Listed everywhere the others are: the viewer's list and the Help menu (Help > Against the field), the Documents directory, the links at the top of every page, the readme's Which guide table; the readme and the About keep a short word and the link.
504. "i think ur problem is u arnt focusing on shit like landmarks on zoom in and are just indefinitly zooming in on nothing". The wheel in the matter now comes down onto what is there at each size: from about six atom spacings it picks the nearest atom, inside the atom its nucleus, inside the nucleus a proton or neutron, inside that a quark (MatterView.Landmarks, called on every notch in; a pick already made at that level or deeper is kept). Rolling in never goes between things into nothing.
505. "not crawl, shit will take eons at a crawl but need to be human viewable this is taking forever u fucked up the speed, and also there is a galaxy type that looks like a solid mass thats too bright and to icony and need fioner edge". The slowness was the test's own PNG writer (330 ms a frame, so the picture crawled while every frame was pressed): the test runs' shots can be BMP (--bmp, written in a few ms) and only once the view is narrower than a size (--shotsbelow <km>). The solid mass was a globular cluster: its stars are now dimmed by how many share a pixel (the galaxies' own crowd factor: the share of the cluster's disc per star against a dot's area, times 0.35, never below 0.03, with the far galaxies' crowd floor at 0.015 and their smallest dot 0.0012), so a cluster is a glow of stars with a soft edge, not a white coin.
506. "u are zooming toi infinity again". The test's wheel (--wheel) now ends on the last landmark, the quark: no notch is rolled once the matter's scale is at or below the smallest anything is known to be (MatterView.KnownKm, 1e-19 m; UniverseView.MatterKm says the scale in the matter, 0 above it). The zoom itself in the game keeps no floor, as 478 asks and the documents say.
507. "wuts happening im just seeing blue flashes at 10 zepto meters and nothing is in focus" / "and wtf its not suppose to be running so fast that people have photo seizures". Two faults. The black: the glide to what was picked never caught up (it closed 2% of the distance a second; the wheel shrank the view 3000 times a second), so the nucleus sat a hundred screens off with its name on the card, and the same for the proton and the quark. Now whatever is picked is held within 0.3 of the picture however fast the wheel goes (the glide eases as before, and what is left past that is pulled in), and at a hundredth of the picture away the view is on it exactly; this also explains the black atom views of the tour's shots, where the tour's own zoom outran the glide. The flashes: the quark's glow was drawn up to three hundred screens wide below 100 zeptometres, and the card dropped it on some frames and kept it on others, a strobe; it is never wider than four screens now (it still fills the view and stays). And nothing in the nucleon flickers: the gluon tubes breathe at 1.5 radians a second (was 9) and the sea's quark pairs last over a second (was a quarter).
508. "need to slow down electrons and querks". Electrons go round at a third of 500's speed (0.05 of the shell's rate, a ninth of the first) and their paths precess at 0.025 radians a second (a turn in about four minutes); quarks turn in their nucleon at a quarter of before (0.07 and 0.05 radians a second, the breathing of their radius at 0.05), so a quark crosses its nucleon in about half a minute.
509. The face's crossing bands at 186 nm, its grid of square tiles at 30 nm ("and just beforee atom view its a weird grid like checkbaord of differnt colors", 484) and the saw along every terrace edge at 4 nm were one cause: the face's per-pixel detail is read from three sheets of the wave tile, and they were laid along the x, y and z axes, so on a level face (or any face square to an axis) two of the three collapsed into stripes along x and along z, which crossed into a grid, quantised into square terraces, and stepped as a saw. The three sheets are now tilted to the diagonals (normals (1,1,1), (-1,1,1), (1,1,-1), normalised), so every face square to an axis reads a full pattern from all three (MeshPipeline WPLANE_A/B, in the single and double paths); and the blend between the tile's stored points is eased (smoothstep) instead of straight, so a terrace's edge bends instead of kinking at every point.
510. "i SAID: next time ill install and test!". No run of the game by me, of any copy with any arguments, from here on: changes go out by release.cmd, the user installs and runs, and sends what they see. A past permission to run a test is spent once used and never carries to the next change.
511. "something is up ist just zooms in and out of the same galzxy a bunch and never does the astropid and comet zoomin and neverr sayw the black hole aven thos it said it was looking at them" / "and its narrating shit thats not on screen". The tour's picks (a black hole, an asteroid, a comet, a star, a world, an atom, a nucleus, a proton, a quark) could find nothing and the tour went on with the old pick, so the next shots framed the galaxy or the star while the captions told of the hole or the comet. Now a pick that finds nothing (after its few seconds of looking) skips every stop that depends on it, up to the next pick: nothing is narrated that is not in view. And the tour writes what it does to the zoom log (%TEMP%\universe_zoom.log, now kept to 4 MB so a whole tour fits): each stop as it begins, with the distance, the pick and in the matter the card and how far the pick sits from the focus; each pick as it is made, or as it fails with why (how many bodies round you, stars, the system, the galaxy). The cause of the failed picks is read from that log on the next run.
512. "at 10 nano meter the planet went invisible and all i see is stars" / "or blackness i dont know". The tour's zoom waited on the ground's backlog (its pieces still being made) at every height, including just above the hand-over to the matter at about two micrometres, where the ground asks for pieces it never finishes; so the wheel stopped a few nanometres above the ground, below where the ground can be drawn, and the matter never took over. Below a centimetre the grains cover the ground and nothing is waited for (TourPace and the waiting flag both), so the zoom goes on down to the hand-over.
513. "and on atom scale some electrons are frozen while others are moving". 508 slowed every electron to a twentieth of the shell's rate, and the outer shell's rate is 1, so a valence electron took two minutes a turn: frozen to the eye, while the inner ones (up to eight times the rate) still moved. The rate is now 0.3 times the square root of the shell's rate: the outer shell a turn in about twenty seconds, the innermost in about seven, every one seen moving and none fast.
514. "i never saw qwerks it mazxed out and said smallest thing we know and i never saw qwerks". The quark pick, the zoom to it and the view at it are logged on the next run (511); what is in the picture at the quark is written to the log with the pick's offset from the focus, so the black is told from the code and the log, not guessed.
515. "and why is GPU using 40% at times but only using 124MD of the 16G of vram". By design: nothing is stored on the card (no textures, no models, no stored art), so its memory holds only the frame, the depth, the wave tile and a few buffers of points, about 110 to 130 MB; every pixel is worked out from equations each frame, which is the 40% busy. The card's memory is not what the program is short of; a card with little memory runs it the same.
516. "aand on the black hole dive fly by do a skim of the scretions disks of gasses and starrs being eaten properly made as i think u have it just as an effect not a real accretion disk of stars and gasses and gravity destoying of stars". It was an effect: a plate of points turned as one piece. Now (every motion on the card, Pipeline's black-hole frame; the parts made once per kind of hole from content/bodies.dnat): the disc's gas, each point on its own Kepler orbit (angle rate 1/r^1.5), so the inner edge goes round many times faster than the rim and the disc shears into spiral streams; its near-light speed (v/c = sqrt(1/(2r))) brightens the side coming toward you and dims the side going away (Doppler beaming, the lopsided disc the Event Horizon Telescope saw), from where the camera is in the disc's own frame; a swarm of stars (swarm-stars, swarm-rs) on tight ellipses round the hole, Kepler's equation solved on the card so each rushes through its close end and crawls through its far; and at feeding holes by eating-share, a star torn apart: stretched along its orbit into a stream (a tidal disruption) that winds in round the hole and feeds the disc, hotter and bluer as it falls, its remains a bright knot at the head, the stream flowing. The disc's reach (disc-rs) and its inner edge's turn as watched (inner-orbit-seconds) are content. The tour's shots at a black hole are set in the disc's own frame (ShotUp: the disc's axis is up), so rise is measured from the disc, and a new stop, skim, grazes across it three degrees above its plane; the disc's caption tells of the orbits and the stars, {hole} of the eaten star.
517. "and u have the expansion of space properly, yeah?? space is always expanding and at 13 buillion light years it expands faster then light can reach the opposite side of the galaxy thats wher the visible universe barrior comes from, so its not really a end all of the universe its the distance light can no longer reach before the vastness of expanding space out paces light speed at full universe scale.. do you need to research this./.. becasue theraretically if u move a light year in any direction u can see 1 light year further of the 15 buillion into the "Edge": of the universe" so it my belirf the univers is infiniate but for the fack that space expands faster than light can move through it" / "but we ater talking billions of years and billions of time from the big bang to wher not a start is visible from any other star,, this is just tesctnical facts and im not sure how to build it in". Right, and it was not so: the universe was a ball of fixed size about the origin (no galaxies made past it, the camera held just inside it). Now there is no edge: galaxies are made wherever you go (SpawnRegion's ball test gone, HoldInside gone), and what you see ends only at your horizon, centred on you, which moves with you (the web's light already faded with distance from the camera; the far light is now reddened on the card by how far it came, the stretch of space). across-ly keeps its meaning as how far you see. The far future (galaxies carried past the horizon until none is seen from another) is not shown: at the fastest clock, ten years a second, a billion years is three years of play; what can be seen of it is the horizon and the reddening, and the horizon's caption says why ({horizon}: dark energy, the Hubble rate, from content/physics.dnat @law cosmos).
518. "and what about dark matter and dark energy to make gravity work?" / "based on their angul;ar moment? just things to think about". Both in content now (@law cosmos: dark-matter-share 0.85, rim-speed-km-s 220, dark-energy-share 0.68, hubble-km-s-mpc 70): a galaxy's card gives its mass with its halo and says its stars orbit at a flat speed out to the rim, held by the dark halo (without it they would slow outward like planets); the web's caption says dark matter's gravity shaped it; the horizon's words say dark energy speeds the stretch. A galaxy's stars do not yet move by their angular momentum in the dark halo's pull (a galaxy turns once in about 200 million years: at ten years a second, nothing to see in play); the speeds are stated, not run.
519. "as i dont thinkg galxies are acutally being held togetther buy the laws of nature. like if i has a setting to turn off all gravity.. would stars explode pressures of all bodies zero and every body move to straight line travel... right?" Right, in nature: without gravity a star's own pressure blows it apart in about an hour (the sound-crossing time of a star of one solar mass), every world and moon leaves on the straight line of its orbit's speed, and a galaxy's stars fly off on their tangents. In the universe here orbits follow Kepler's law as a rule (each body placed on its ellipse by the clock), not as a pull integrated step by step; galaxies are patterns of stars, not bodies in each other's pull; a star's shape is its template's. A gravity switch is buildable from the speeds already known (each body's orbital speed is worked out: with gravity off it would carry on straight at that speed, visible in a second of play at a month a second; a star would swell at its sound speed, visible at an hour a second); noted as open, not built.
520. "i think it might be a focus problem" / "as last test it zoomed in a bunch never focusing and was saying it was looking at a astraoid and commet when just in black space of a mix of stars". Two framing faults, both in what the shot came to rest on. The black hole: the pick weighed a feeding hole a millionfold nearer, so a feeding heart ten million light-years off outscored the quiet heart of the galaxy you were at, and the hole shots flew out of the galaxy with the caption saying they were going down to its heart; the pick now takes only a hole within a few widths of the galaxy you are in (this galaxy's own heart, a feeding one before a quiet one). The asteroid and the comet: the shot came in from wherever the camera happened to be, and a rock lit from its star on the far side is black against black, so the card named an asteroid that was in the picture and unseen; a shot of a small body now comes round to its lit side, the sweep's middle facing its star.
521. "the astoid trails off to the sids and focus doent follow it i never saew it upclose" / "aand to comet caought the dark side of it" / "or its just not bright enough". Three faults.
