The camera kept riding along with the planet it had left: each frame the shot placed it at the asteroid, then the planet's motion shifted it, a constant offset that is nothing far out and ten degrees up close, so the asteroid slid off to the side as the camera came in; with an asteroid, comet or black hole picked (none of them moves) the camera rides with nothing. 520's lit-side framing swapped the shot's start direction, which is a jump of the camera at the shot's start, and the comet still came in on its dark side; now the shot's sweep itself carries the camera round from where it is to the body's lit side, the short way, so it comes to rest with the star behind the camera. And a comet's nucleus was drawn at its true darkness (albedo 0.04, among the darkest bodies there are), black against the stars; it is shown as a camera exposing for it sees it, mid-grey, as Rosetta saw 67P.
522. "and it keeps having (s)'s stand alone when prlulizing shit like ur add errnonious spaces". The cards' font (IconFont, our own blocky letters) had no apostrophe, so "light's wavelength" was drawn "LIGHT S WAVELENGTH" and "proton's" "PROTON S": the possessive's s standing alone after a gap. The apostrophe is in the font now, with the quote marks, the semicolon, the exclamation and question marks and the equals sign a card may carry.
523. "and u still using Sun everyehwer in the write ups and texts is solar mass and shiot not Sun there is no Sun in this Unioverse". Every mass, width and brightness in the cards and the content is now in solar units (solar masses, solar radii, solar luminosities), and every kind of thing once called "Sun-like" is a star of about one solar mass (the content's says, stage, name and example fields; the cards of stars, black holes and galaxies; the deep view's heart; the builders' guide). Our own star's name stays only where the real one is meant: the real Milky Way's and the real Solar System's entries, the real planets' notes, and the lines that say only our own star is the Sun.
524. "and why are ther ever only oxygen atoms on atom view" / "maybe its the luck of the draw but seems excessive to alway land on owygen". Not luck: oxygen is three atoms in five of every silicate (olivine, pyroxene, feldspar, quartz) and two in three of ice, and a face's top layer is mostly its oxygen, so the nearest atom was oxygen every time. The atom pick now takes the mineral's elements in turn (each pick the next element the face has, sorted, from the pick count and the seed) and the nearest atom of that element: olivine gives magnesium, oxygen and silicon by turns, ice hydrogen and oxygen. For the wheel's landmark pick and the tour's alike.
525. "and u need to acutally do the fly around and have the ability to fly through the atoms and elctrons and around the scale a little bit more as u kinda just zoom in only when these scales have reall shit to fly around and u may need to upscale the atoms a little bit so when u coom into them they dont instany got to proton nutron till a orbit and rotate of the atom vies like galaxctic and solar system views fly arounds should be". The tour's atom was eight seconds of a slow arc and then a straight zoom to the nucleus. Now it is flown round as a galaxy or a system is: round the atom twice as long and further round (sixteen seconds at fourteen degrees a second, closing in slowly), then in among its electrons with the view circling as it goes (a zoom with a pan: the pan now works in the matter, where the eye goes round the point you dive into), then an orbit through the inner shells for twelve seconds, and only then down to the nucleus; the bond crossing, the electron, the nucleus, the proton and the quark are each held longer. The atoms are not scaled up: they keep their real sizes, and the time spent at them is what grew. / "maybe do some rotating too so dep depths of zoom show everything is similar even behind your view": the turn among the crystal's atoms and the turn through the inner shells are now full turns (twenty-four seconds at fifteen degrees a second), and the nucleus three quarters round, so at each depth the view comes all the way round and what is behind you is seen to be the same as what is ahead. / "atom scale i mean": the turn round the atom itself is a full one too (twenty-six seconds at fourteen degrees a second, closing in slowly), before the turn among its electrons. And the readme's zoom table said you were held inside the visible universe outward; it says there is no edge, only your horizon, which moves with you.
526. "okay but do we really have the full table of elements mix on all bodies to what they should have mixes of,, its not just silica a planet is made out of earth has like 118 elements weve found on it". No: the matter was twenty-five minerals of about fifteen elements (oxygen, silicon, aluminium, the alkali and alkaline-earth metals, iron, titanium, sulphur, carbon, hydrogen, nitrogen, chlorine), and the other hundred lived only on the element cards. Real minerals carry most of the natural elements as traces, in place of a host atom on a share of its sites, and that is in the content now (minerals.dnat trace lines, real mineral chemistry): nickel, manganese, chromium and cobalt in olivine in place of magnesium; strontium, barium, rubidium, caesium and lead in the feldspars in place of their alkali; titanium, vanadium, chromium, nickel and zinc in the iron oxides; nickel, cobalt, copper, silver, gold and platinum in pyrite in place of iron and arsenic, selenium, tellurium, antimony, thallium and mercury in place of sulphur; bromine and iodine in halite; the rare earths, zirconium, niobium, hafnium and tantalum in ilmenite and the melt; and the sea's dissolved ions (chlorine, sodium, magnesium, sulphur, calcium, potassium, carbon, bromine, boron, strontium, fluorine, lithium, iodine, uranium, gold) each in a water molecule's place at its real share, the sea's salt now carried as traces at the real one ion in a hundred molecules. Each site's atom is decided from its own hash, so a trace atom is fixed in place: zoom into olivine and one atom in three hundred is nickel; into pyrite and one in three hundred thousand is gold. The mineral's card lists its traces with their shares, and a trace atom's card says what it stands in for and on about one site in how many. Not yet: the bodies themselves by their own bulk make-up (a metal asteroid of iron and nickel, a star's layers), which stays on the roadmap (486).
527. "fiftene elements? wtf did we build the periodic table of elements for u retard" / "and it needs to be proper and have a way to open it". The rest of the natural elements are placed where they really sit: the melt carries tin, molybdenum, tungsten, indium, bismuth, beryllium, thorium, uranium, the whole run of the rare earths and the platinum metals at their real parts per billion; pyrite the platinum metals and rhenium; the sea its uranium's whole decay chain (thorium, radium, radon, polonium, protactinium, actinium, francium, astatine, each at its real, vanishing share); the air its noble gases (neon, helium, krypton, xenon at their real shares on every kind of world with air, and radon as a kind of gas). Every element from hydrogen to uranium now stands somewhere in the matter except technetium and promethium, which are not found in nature. And the periodic table itself, the view that shows all 118 as they really are (each a lump, a drop or a glowing gas at the temperature and pressure you set, a click for its details), was only reachable as a sample set; it opens from the universe now (View > The periodic table), over the world, which waits where you were; Escape or the menu again comes back.
528. "asnd the readme for the repos need a proper first thing releases link and wording and write ups". Both readmes open, before anything else, with the release: a link to the latest release on GitHub and to the same on the lab's Git, then a short write-up with the version, direct links to the installer and the zip (with their size), what it needs, what it weighs installed, and what you get.
529. "okay i hope on next install we can get a finished clip so finger s crosesed" / "and we will need screen shots of like the docs viewer and install requirments and installer posints but not alot of screen shots just the main things" / "and landing page" / "to put in the readmes propely". The 04:27 run's clip was finished (closed normally, 660 s, saved), and its log told the whole tour: every pick and stop worked (comet, worlds, land, the grains, the lattice, the atom, the nucleus, the three quarks at 200 attometres) but one, the black hole at a Dark galaxy, which found nothing among 169 bodies. The pick now goes straight to the galaxy's own heart (every galaxy has one at its middle; found among the bodies round you, or made on the spot from its place, the same one the galaxy would get), with the distance and the galaxy's scale logged if even that fails. A safety exit (the watchdog) now finishes a clip being filmed first, so a clip is never left without its index. Nine frames of that clip are in both readmes as the screens of the universe as it is (galaxy, web, a gas giant, a comet, a world, the ground, the lattice, the nucleus, the quarks), 1280 wide, with their captions; the landing page, the installer and the document viewer are windows of the user's own screen, grabbed when they are open. And "a up quark" is "an up quark".
530. "and im still not seeing a loading bar just black scrren on load in after seed input and tour start". Two causes. The universe's making begins the moment the seed is given, before the universe can draw its own loading screen, so those seconds were a black window: a loading screen is now drawn as the very first frame, before the making starts. And the loading screen itself was a near-black fill with a bar six pixels tall and small writing, which read as a black screen for the few seconds it stood: it now says MAKING THE UNIVERSE in big letters, with a bar half the screen wide, its percentage beside it, and what is being made written under it.
531. "when it moves away from the comet and astroid it doesnt slowly pan towrds the next target jit just instally zooms making them disappear instanlty instead of a smothe pan away before zooming". 499's departure pace was measured from the distance to the shot's subject, and the comet is picked before the shot to it starts, so at the asteroid the pace began an AU wide and the asteroid was gone in a frame. The pace now begins from the nearest thing drawn where the shot starts (the asteroid or comet you were beside, counting the bodies drawn close round you), so it pulls away and shrinks at the stop's max-rate, tenfolds a second, while the look glides round to the next subject.
532. "and whit it looks up to the sky from ground level it should comit on atmospher if one and how it changes athe color of light and its composition which it already has" / "and the star color". The sky caption ({sky}, on the look up and the sky stops) now says, after what is up there: the air's make-up (the world's own, its first gases with their shares), what it does to the light (a clear nitrogen or oxygen air scatters the blue, so the sky is its colour by day and reddens toward the horizon where the light crosses the most air; a hazy or dusty air tints the sky its colour), and the star's colour from its surface heat (blue-white, white, yellow-white, yellow-orange, orange, red by its kelvins), with how it shows through that air; on an airless world, that the sky is black by day with the stars beside the star and its light unchanged. The sky's colour is the air's own colour as the game draws it, in words.
533. "is it normal the gpu only ever uses 124mb of 16 gb?vram? cant we make it better if we use more of the vram? and at 500 nanmoeter is pasues in eroor or crashed" / "it never went into atoms and qwerks". The 04:57 run's log: on a rocky world the land came down on an ice sheet (water ice, which has no grains laid out: a level face), the spot pick found nothing in its five seconds, and every stop below depended on it, so the crystal, the atom, the nucleus, the proton and the quark were all skipped (511), each pick waiting its five seconds first: forty seconds standing at two micrometres with the captions changing, which read as a pause or an error, and then out. A spot or grain pick with nothing laid out to pick now takes the spot right under the point you dive into, as a click there would, so the crystal and all below it follow on an ice sheet or the sea as on a grain. The card's memory: the 124 MB is all the program keeps on the card (the frame, the depth, the wave tile, a few buffers of points); more memory would not make it faster, because nothing waits on memory: what waits is the processor making the ground's pieces at the grains (515), and the way to that is making them on the card, which stays on the roadmap.
534. "do .exe run on linix.. how does linix people install?" / "okay well make sure that path is real and its documented propley for how linix people can use the app with out crazy workarounds" / "make sure all the docs are proper with this requuest and fixes". No: the program is a Windows one (Direct3D 12 and Win32 by hand, built with the C# compiler inside Windows), and Linux had no path at all. The one plain path is Wine, which runs Windows programs and carries Direct3D 12 on Vulkan, and it is now one script: the zip carries run-on-linux.sh at its root (linux/run.sh in the project), which checks for Wine 8 or newer and a Vulkan driver (printing the install line for the distribution when one is missing), finds the exe beside it, sets a private Wine folder of its own so nothing of the user's is touched, and runs it; the first run may offer Wine Mono, its .NET, to say yes to. The page On Linux (docs/release/UNIVERSE-LINUX.md; docs/LINUX.md in Universe and in the zips) gives the short way, the Steam way (Proton, the best Direct3D 12 on Linux), what the script checks, and where it stands: the program uses nothing Wine does not carry (a Direct3D 12 device at feature level 11, a flip-model swap chain, Win32 windows and menus, .NET 4), and the card's-memory query that Wine may not have is already allowed to fail; but it has not yet been run on a Linux desktop by us, which the page says, with how to send the Wine log if it fails. Linked from every page's navigation, the documents table, the readmes' release blocks, the technical guide's requirements, the comparison's needs, DNA-AND's setup guide and the roadmap (the native Linux build stays there: Vulkan, X11 and Wayland, a Linux C# runtime).
535. "so everything is basicly done now ? no in progress items for the universe project not completed" / "i think u havent been checking them off and properly marking things still needed to be done". Right: 119 lines sat at in progress and dozens at open that were built and shipped, with "waits on the test" on them. Every built item is ticked and written to FINALIZED in its words (107, then six more, then the universe preview release's own tree: the one world, the release, the Universe project, the readmes and credits, Windows and Linux installs, running on anyone's card; the fixes the user's runs and clip showed: no space at 20 micrometres, no towers, no time-out at the hand-over, the quark seen). The roadmap's stages are marked as they stand: R1, R2, R4.4, R4.5, R5 and R16 done, R3 and R4 in progress (save files, frames in flight, the ground on the card), R17 in progress (the 55 image slots wait on the game), R18 open. The readme's clip is now the grand tour as the game recorded it on the user's own run (the 04:27 clip was gone, deleted by the installer as an old clip; the 04:57 one: the cosmic web, a galaxy, a comet, a world, its ground, the face of a grain), 420 wide, 3.4 MB, and the social images of both repositories are built on its frames and posted again. The user's own screenshot showed a galaxy's card drawn over the frame counter and the stats at the top right, both garbled: a pick's card never lands there now, it goes under them. What stays open for the universe, in the TODO in the user's words: the screens of the landing page, the installer and the document viewer (their windows), the Linux path run on a Linux desktop, every body's own make-up (R18), a gravity switch, the two engine stages (the ground and everything else on the card), and the laters (the rest of relativity, light below its wavelength).
536. "and wtf fuck are u using to make these clips 500 mb for minutes,, wtf are u using f2000 instead of f16 or something". The clips are H.264 through Windows' own Media Foundation, but asked for at sixty frames a second and twelve megabits a second at 1080p (six bits a pixel), which is a broadcast rate: 90 MB a minute, 524 MB for the six-minute tour. A clip is now thirty frames a second (every other frame of the game's sixty, a frame within 28 ms of the last let go, the timing kept) at six megabits a second for 1080p (three bits a pixel, never below 2.5 or above 16 megabits), so stars stay sharp and a minute weighs about 45 MB, a six-minute tour about 270 MB. The in-game readme says so by the F9 key.
537. "it should only make one clip as long as its recording and not a a gig file so use something appropriate that doesnt fuck our c/9 waveform into hevey picxel style files that are fucking massive". One file per recording it already was (F9 to F9, or the whole tour). The weight was the rate control: the encoder was asked for a fixed flood of bits every second whether the frame was black space or a star field. It is now set, through its own knobs (ICodecAPI by way of the sink writer, a VARIANT by hand), to rate control by quality, 70 of 100: bits go only where the picture has detail, so black space costs nothing, the stars and the grain's face keep their edges, and a clip weighs what its pictures hold, a few tens of MB a minute at 1080p; the six megabits of 536 stays as the ceiling, and an encoder without the knob keeps that rate. The MP4 is H.264 through Windows' own Media Foundation, which is the right container for a clip anyone can play; the waveform is what the game draws, and a quality-ruled encoder keeps it instead of smearing it to blocks.
538. "and ther should be a REC notice if recording liek carasvideos tapes have". While a clip records, a red dot blinking once a second, REC and the clip's length (minutes and seconds) sit at the top middle of the screen on a dark plate, as a camcorder's viewfinder shows them. It is drawn after the frame's picture has been taken for the clip (the card can now take the frame mid-way, CaptureHere, with the copy itself factored out of EndFrame), so it shows on screen and never on the tape; a screenshot taken while recording stays clean the same way. The in-game readme says so by the F9 key.
539. "okay i took alot of image shghotts of all the fproblems" / "check images for scalle and visual issues" / "to fix issues at those scales". Twenty-one shots at a grain of calcite, 500 nanometres down to 200 picometres. Two faults, both at the face. The rain of fine lines down every face at every size: the three sheets of pattern the face's detail is read from, tilted this morning (509), had two sheets sharing an axis, so on faces at many tilts two of them collapsed into stripes at once; the sheets' frame is orthonormal now (each sheet's axes are the other two sheets' normals) and each sheet counts by how squarely it faces the surface (the surface's direction from the grain's middle, the standard triplanar weighting), so a sheet seen edge-on, which can only draw stripes, counts for nothing. The floating slabs with black between them from 20 nanometres down: the grains round the one you are on are drawn as coarse stones, and closer than a twentieth of the grain their coarse bodies, which stand off their true shape by far more than the view, poked through the face as slabs; closer than that the face and its atoms are all that is drawn. The dark blobs are the iron-oxide nanoparticles that really sit on a face (5 to 50 nm), and the trace atom met at 200 picometres was strontium in calcium's place, as the content gives it.
540. "another thing on atom scall the orbits of attoms ar never constant they make one orbit then the orbitapaths changes.. do you not know this did you do your electro research" / "and on atom view i want you to dive into a electrons travels folling one on its orbiit at close randge, might need to effect scale so getting to thhat zoom actual makes electrons bigger like planets on view up close befoe wtitching to the nucleus" / "with pan and rotate properly" / "liek its a solar system of its own and like u are following a comet but an electron". Known, and now shown: an electron has no path of its own, only a cloud of where it may be, so the loop it is drawn on changes every time round (its turn and tilt from the count of times round, each electron its own, eased in over the last quarter of the way round, never a jump), with the slow turning of 500 besides, and the path's marks show the loop of this time round. The tour now dives to one electron as to a comet (a zoom to 25 femtometres' view with the view circling as it goes), and rides beside it for sixteen seconds while the view circles, the electron shown as a glowing body a share of the view (a point has no size anyone has found; this is the size we give it, as the caption says), the nucleus a spark far off, before the nucleus is picked.
541. "look at all the scrreen shots all the shit above 2 nano meter dirrectly is all fucked" / "check the clip i made" / "the zoom to an electro shows nothingtill the nucleas in erorr" / "the clip of the patterneing in the first solar system fly by and do you not see the warp like effect of stars at a focal point as it zooms out.. it looks trashy but i know its accurate just looks weird maybe do something smart so that warping focal effect isnt so heavy" / "they almost look like a grid not natural solar system". The 05:48 clip, frame by frame against its log. The pattern round the star and the asteroid: the ring drawn round what is picked was a ring of dashes, which read as a ring of bodies; it is a thin, faint, unbroken circle now. The warp of stars toward one point as the view pulls out: a glow that would reach past a third of the screen had its corners pulled in toward the middle, so every near star became a sliver pointing at the screen's middle; such a glow is now shrunk whole and dimmed with it, so a star you pass close fades as it goes by. The black from three nanometres down to half a nanometre: the face is no longer drawn there and each atom was only a nucleus point and electron dots too small to see; between the face and the atom's own system every atom is now a ball of its element's colour at its bonded size with the bonds between them, the ball-and-stick model, and the electron dots are larger. The electron ride that showed nothing: the riding (the view following the electron, its larger glow) was in one of the two ways an atom is drawn and not the other, which the ice's hydrogen atoms use; it is in both now, and every atom's electrons can be picked whichever way it is drawn.
542. "and pressing the start page button in the menu in correctly closes and restart s the whole application". It started a second copy of the program and closed this one. It now comes back to the start page in the same window: this universe is let go, the page is shown again, the seed it asks for (or a new one) makes a new universe, and the tour starts if asked, with the loading screen shown while it is made.
543. "fix it all and did you see the solar system patterning". Seen, in the clip's frames at 1.3 AU: the asteroid belt was three thousand dots each a fixed share of the view, which from afar added up to a solid white annulus with the orbit lines as rings inside it, a machined pattern round the star; the Kuiper belt and the Oort cloud the same farther out. A real belt is rubble spread thin across a huge ring, a haze from afar. The belts' and orbit lines' points are fainter now and smaller, and the whole set is dimmed by how many points share the belt's patch of screen (its ring's area against the dots' area, as a galaxy's stars are dimmed by their crowding), so from afar the belt is a faint haze and close in its stones stand alone.

Not the hand-over: the log shows the tour standing on the to-grains stop from 06:15:56 to 06:19:57, its whole four-minute limit, a centimetre over the ground with the grain overlay just on, then going on to the grain. The click that brought the window to the front never pauses the tour (by design: only a deliberate click does), but it still went through as a pick, and the tour's target became a spot on the ground off to one side of where it looked. The tour only moves where it looks, and coming down onto a spot the angle between the look (down) and the spot (under you, a little to one side) grows as the height shrinks, so at a centimetre it passed the limit and the zoom went to zero; the stall watch never fired because the ground's queue is never empty down there. Three fixes: the activating click picks nothing; a spot on the ground counts as on course within about thirty degrees of the look; and below the overlay the ground's queue no longer holds the stall watch. The 12 frames a second with it: the ground kept splitting its pieces on down toward microbe size under the overlay, a queue of eleven hundred pieces in flight for detail the grains already cover (the land's own shape stops at a quarter of a grain; the grains are drawn over the ground from a centimetre and as the matter below). The ground's pieces now split no finer than four grains to an edge.

In the matter a click picked only what lay within twenty-four pixels of it, else the spot on the surface under it, and looking out across a grain's face or up there was no surface under the click, so nothing was picked. A click always picks something now: the thing it is on; else the nearest thing within a sixth of the screen of it; else the spot on the surface under it; else the point one view ahead along the line under the mouse, which is then dived into as any spot is.

Every body has its make-up now, on its card and under the zoom. A small body draws its kind from the content (content/small-bodies.dnat): an asteroid of the belt by the kinds' belt-share at its place in the belt (stony S-type inside, carbon-rich C-type outside, metal M, enstatite E, basalt V rare, dark icy P at the outer edge), a Kuiper body, a comet's nucleus (four parts dust to one of ice), a rogue planet; each kind has its mix of minerals (content/minerals.dnat, with the new kamacite, taenite, troilite, graphite, ammonia ice and carbon monoxide ice, each on its real cell), its make-up in words, its density (so its mass, its pull and escape speed) and its albedo, and the card lists its minerals with their shares, the elements they hold by count of atoms, and the traces down to parts per billion from every mineral's real trace chemistry. At its surface the zoom goes on into its own grains and atoms as a world's ground does (the same matter view, its up from the body's middle, its star's light), and the wheel comes down onto an atom, its nucleus, a nucleon, a quark. A star's card gives what it is made of by count of atoms (content/stars.dnat makeup: the young kinds the solar photosphere's shares, each star its own share of everything past helium, the long-lived K and M dwarfs spread lower as many are old), the evolved kinds their own (a red giant's nitrogen dredged up, a Wolf-Rayet's bare helium, a carbon star's soot, a white dwarf's hydrogen skin over carbon and oxygen), and the dive into its surface is its own plasma. A neutron star's surface is dived into as its crust: iron nuclei in a lattice a hundredth of an atom's width apart, electrons racing between them. The tour dives onto an asteroid and down to its atoms, and back out (a zoom stop can end once back out of the matter: out: yes).

Round every hole, quiet or feeding, its gravity well is drawn in space: the dip of space round a mass (Flamm's paraboloid, its depth 2 root(rs (r - rs)), the real shape), rings and spokes from the horizon out to forty radii, and at the horizon a sharp step straight down to a point. With a hole picked and near, and all through the fall, a graph of the well sits at the right of the screen with a mark where you are: on the curve outside, down the step inside. Zooming in at the shadow goes through the horizon: pitch black (no light from anything in here reaches anything else), the card giving the escape speed where you are (exactly light's at the horizon, more inside, less outside, as the user says), your distance from the middle, your own time left to it (a radial fall from rest, the same in Newton's and Einstein's gravity: two thirds of r^1.5 over the root of 2GM; from the horizon of a four-million-solar-mass hole, about 26 seconds) and the tide head to foot, until at the quarks' size the singularity: quarks pressed into a ball that stays the same size however far in you go (a point), picked like any quark, the card saying what the laws we have can and cannot say of it. The tour falls in after the horizon shot and climbs back out.
549. "are yuou sure every star type has the exasct same make up??" / "doesnt color and temp depend on matter with in it and its pressures based on matter in the star?" / "tpya and amount" / "type*". Checked against what is known: a star's colour and heat come from its mass, not its make-up. The same gas (about nine atoms in ten hydrogen, one in ten helium, a little of everything else) makes a red dwarf and a blue giant; the heavier the star, the harder its core is squeezed and the hotter it burns, and that sets its surface's heat and colour. So the young kinds (O through M) share one make-up in the content, each star with its own share of the heavier elements (a third to three times its kind's, the long-lived dwarfs lower), and the kinds that differ really do differ: the evolved stars (giants, Wolf-Rayet, carbon stars), the dead ones (white dwarfs, neutron stars). The card says so.

The research (the papers are listed in the session's report): a galaxy of a hundred billion stars has two to five supernovae a century (core collapse about two, white-dwarf about one; from other galaxies' counts, the massive-star birth rate, aluminium-26 and the pulsar birth rate), so across the observable universe several go off every second, not one a day; a core-collapse supernova shines at about five hundred million solar luminosities (absolute magnitude -17) for a hundred-day plateau, a white-dwarf one at four billion (-19.3), up in eighteen days and halving every twelve after; the matter flies out at five to ten thousand km/s (a few hundredths of light's speed, the outer layers up to a tenth), free for a few hundred years, then slowed as it sweeps up the gas between the stars (the radius as the two-fifths power of the age), a shell seen for about a hundred thousand years, not millions, until it merges with that gas; the glowing face (the photosphere) is about ten billion km across and about 5,500 K on a plateau, a hundred thousand degrees at the shock's breakout, drawing back in as the fireball thins to a cloud. Built so, all of it from the content (content/physics.dnat @law supernova and the six @supernova kinds: II-P, II-L, IIn, Ib/Ic, Ia, superluminous, each with its share, light curve, heat, ejecta speed and what it leaves): every galaxy's supernovae come from its seed and the game's time, at its own stars; each is drawn as bright as physics says from where you are (its light over the square of its distance against a star of one solar luminosity ten light-years off, the glow growing with the logarithm of that: in its own galaxy it outshines every star, as a real one does, and from another galaxy it is one point among that galaxy's, never shining through everything); close in it is the fireball at its real size in the colour of its heat, with the ejecta's thin edge racing out ahead, then the thinning cloud with its remnant inside; in the galaxy you are in the shells of the old ones stand at their sizes by their ages, and at each one's middle a neutron star (a star of eight to about twenty solar masses; the youngest spinning tens of times a second, slowing as they age), a black hole (heavier) or a magnetar (a superluminous one's), among the bodies round you, their cards saying when and from what. The star that died is gone from the stars round you and no system opens at it: its worlds went with it (the inner ones vaporised, the outer flung loose as the star's mass left), and its neighbours' cards say what the shock did when it passed (within a couple of light-years the air stripped and the faces scorched, within ten the ozone gone and life on the exposed sides killed, out to thirty the sky lit as by a second star). In a few million years that gas condenses into new stars and worlds, as the cards say; the game's clock reaches that at its fastest setting. A supernova going now can be clicked, and the tour flies to the nearest one in whatever stage it is in (rising, on its plateau, fading, on its tail: each run its own kind and stage) and circles it.
551. The rule the content reader lives by, written down (docs/CONTENT-FORMAT.md): a comment inside an entry is only a comment; a blank line ends the entry. And the tour's new zoom key: out: yes, a zoom out that ends once back out of the matter (a body's ground, a hole) into space.

The checks are sourced at the foot of the page. The sizes are current: 1.4 MB installed (2.1 MB with the uninstaller), 0.57 MB zipped, 0.68 MB installer, 0.29 MB of templates.

Now: every hole's shadow is drawn black by the law for where the camera stands (sin^2 = (27/4)(rs/r)^2 (1 - rs/r): 2.6 radii across from afar, half the sky at the photon sphere, nearly all of it just outside the horizon, so zooming in and out shows the horizon's curvature growing as it must); the disc's own light is bent on the card as its own thing (the far side lifted into an arch over and under the shadow, its lift growing with how far past the hole it lies; what lies within the shadow's cone behind the hole captured and dropped), and the background's ring is left to light from well behind the hole; the marker glow fades out within a hundred radii; the beaming is kept lopsided but never a missing half; the stream winds the disc's way; the swarm's stars and the jets are thin. Looked up (the sources are in the session's report): the picture is Flamm's paraboloid, the still slice of Schwarzschild space as a surface, a funnel flat far out and vertical at the horizon's throat, its depth 2 root(rs (r - rs)); the rubber sheet is its everyday cousin; inside the horizon the falling observer's own slice is a cone, which does run on in. Built so, in the world: inside the horizon the sheet of space round the hole is drawn as a square grid, bent into Flamm's funnel outside the throat and into the infaller's cone inside it, down to the point; you are on the axis at your depth, the walls round you, every line of the grid running down to the singularity ahead, the horizon's ring bright above you; the axis is the way you came in, fixed in space, so turning the view turns round the well; it is never bent by the hole's lens. The graph on screen stays as the numbers.
559. "like i said it should be u looking at the horizon not at the singularity as u enter". You come in looking back at the horizon's ring now (the look set up the axis at the dive, on the tour or by hand), and on the tour the view turns over as you fall, down the well's wall, slowly round so the grid wheels past, to face the point only near the end. In a dive the zoom goes on wherever you look.
560. "not just a random bend actual physics". The inner cone had been a number of mine. Now the whole surface is the metric's: outside the horizon Flamm's, its height above the throat 2 root(rs (r - rs)) (from z'^2 = g_rr - 1 = rs/(r - rs)), a bowl flat far out and vertical at the throat; inside the horizon the same equation with its sign flipped, as it must be there (g_rr is negative: space and time have swapped roles, and the depth is timelike), 2 root(rs (rs - r)), running from the throat down to the point at r = 0, two radii below it. You are at your real r on it (the view's own distance from the middle against the horizon's radius), the far sheet above and behind you, the throat's ring at depth zero, the point ahead. The free-faller's own slices of this space are flat, so what is drawn is the still slice's shape, the one the pictures show.

It is both now: from the horizon to the middle the camera itself travels down the axis it came in on, always at the view's own distance from the middle, the scale shrinking with it, so the well's grid, the card's distance and the camera's place in space agree at every moment; climbing out it comes back up the same way and leaves the dive just outside the horizon.
564. "so the travel speed to the singularity and all of that need to be right just zooming into the micoscropt on the event horizon doesnt get you to the singularity". The fall runs by the law now: from rest far away, r^1.5 shrinks at a steady rate in your own time (dr/dtau = -c root(rs/r)), so from the horizon r(tau) = rs (1 - tau/tauEnd)^(2/3), with tauEnd two thirds of rs over c: 26 seconds for a four-million-solar-mass hole, 70 microseconds for a stellar one, eleven minutes for a hundred-million-sun one. The camera travels down the axis on that clock and the view follows r; the whole is shown over about thirty seconds, slowed or sped as that needs, and the card says by how much, with your own time since the horizon, the time left, and your speed inward (light's own at the horizon, more inside). Past a thousandth of a radius the time left is a hundred-thousandth of the whole, and from there the zoom carries on at its own pace to the quarks' size.

The allowance was tiny too, paced from the nearest thing drawn (a few hundred km off, never in view) rather than from the view. The start is kept in the world's own frame now (zero at the start, growing along the glide), the pace comes from what is in view, and a shot only goes forward.

Every document read top to bottom (the readmes, the release and in-game pages, the guides, the comparison, the setup and build guides, the content format, the lists, the roadmap, the TODO, the FINALIZED, the builder wiki and its seventeen system pages, the player wiki's thirty-five pages, the Linux script, the licence and the privacy note) and measured against the build of today. The comparison stands: the others' numbers are their own published ones, each linked, and the ticks are honest both ways. Fixed: the sizes (the installer is 0.68 MB, the zip 0.58, the program 1.07, installed 1.4; two pages had said 1.7 MB installed), the line counts (19,600 lines of C# and shader in 61 files, 10,900 of templates in 30; the big files 4,000, 2,100 and 1,560 lines), the tour's length (a quarter of an hour, not five minutes), the clip's size choice everywhere a clip is mentioned, the fall through the horizon and the supernova in the readme's tour table, the one-picture-a-level rule in How it works, the sample viewer's menu where the universe's was meant, the builder wiki's "no game code exists yet" and its all-draft legend (now verified, partial, draft and stale per page, the retired toolchain marked stale), the universe view's row (it goes the whole way now; "an edge" is a horizon), the well's dropped graph, the packaging page (the installer, the zip, both release pages, the Universe project, the Wine script), the content format's missing files (bodies, universe, the tour) and the Universe project's `.unt`, the lists' seeded column (geology and climate), the DNA-AND build guide's source layout (what exists and what is planned), the setup guide's first launch (the preview's landing page, not the main menu) and its disk size, the player wiki's wheel line, its FAQ (scratch-made with Windows' compiler; a universe of 100 billion galaxies; Linux through Wine today), its black hole definition and every page's controls tooltip (from the cosmic web to the quarks), the Universe credits' roster and the supernova sources, the pick targets and the zoom's `through` and `pan` in the guide, the roadmap's R19 record, and twenty-odd TODO markers the code had overtaken (listed in FINALIZED), with the continents-do-not-move and day-and-night notes brought up to date.

Added: "The two projects" at the top of the roadmap, each project's what-it-is and what-it-entails in the creator's own words, where each lives, which docs each carries, and that life is DNA-AND's alone; R21, the Universe program's own road (a star's layers, gravity as a live force and the switch, the rest of relativity, decay and the fields, light below its wavelength, the waveform everywhere, native Linux and Mac, the screens, the approved clip), each item with its words; and a public "Still to come" in the Universe readme, with How it works brought to the same list. 575. "yeas the Universe need its own .claude and its own files in it file directory within the danand project" / "so pull the ual repo templet and buuild it all out correctly while kiiping its docs intact" / "not over write existing shit" / "and propelry finalize all the nuniverse todo work weve done in finalizxed makeing sure the todo is only certified still open items" / "proper to each project". The Unity AI Lab `.claude/` template was installed into `Universe/` from the lab's Forgejo (UnityAILab/UAL-ClaudeWorkflow, main, over HTTPS), with the project config, the identity and the public-ok marker as DNA-AND has them; the Universe's `.gitignore` keeps only the personal files out, as DNA-AND's does (the repos are public, `.claude/` included). The Universe got its own workflow files, all new, nothing existing written over: `docs/DESIGN.md` (its pitch, rules and decisions in the creator's words, each pointing to the entry here where it was made), `docs/ROADMAP.md` (what the project is; U1 to U11 as built; U12, its own road), `docs/TODO.md`, `docs/FINALIZED.md`, `docs/DECOMPOSED.md`, `docs/WORK-LAWS.md` (DNA-AND's laws as they apply to the program), and a builder wiki (`wiki/`: the schema, the index, the log, eleven system pages verified against the synced code). `universe-sync.cmd` no longer writes over the Universe's `.gitignore` and never touches its `.claude/`, `wiki/` or workflow files. The ledgers made proper to each project: every finished item of DNA-AND's TODO (371 of them, built and shipped) moved to its FINALIZED word for word with the section it stood in, so the TODO holds only what is certified still open; the Universe's TODO holds only its docs upkeep and U12, its built-and-tested items in its own FINALIZED.

The well inside the horizon had been drawn with its width in the hole's own radii against your scale (the mouth ten billion views wide once you were deep) and only a short stub of rings below you, so looking up was a wall spreading away and looking down a short opening. It is drawn in the fall's own measure now, the dilation itself: each tenfold of the radius two views of length, T tenfolds from the horizon to the singularity at the quarks' size (about twenty-five for a stellar hole, thirty for a supermassive one), so the funnel is far longer than the space the hole takes up; its radius a view and a half round you at every depth and shrinking straight to nothing at the point, the mouth's ring at the top with Flamm's sheet flaring out above it. The whole cone is drawn at every depth: looking down you see the run all the way to the point, looking up the run all the way back to the opening, and the fall down it is the long way it is.
577. "rtemebre time speeds up further from gravity and slows whenin very high gravity and maybe even stops" / "its why we have time dialtion codes for satilights further from gravity time runs differnt". Gravitational time dilation, everywhere you are: the title now gives your own clock against the clocks far from every mass (the system's star, the world you are at by its size and kind, the holes near you: dtau/dt = root(1 - sum 2GM/(r c^2))), slower the deeper you are in a well, stopped at a horizon, and past it "where no far clock sees it". A world's card says how many microseconds a day its own pull slows clocks on its ground against far away and how many of them a satellite four radii up runs faster than the ground, the correction every satellite clock carries. The fall's card says the far watcher never sees you cross the horizon while your own clock runs on to the middle. The cards had already given the star's pull on a world's clocks and a hole's "to a stop at its horizon".

Every use of our own star's name swept out of the content, the code's comments and text, the builder and player wikis, the public pages and the design record's own prose (97 of them: "the Sun's mass", "Sun-like", "a Sun", "in Suns", a system kind named after it, two content keys); the creator's own quoted words stay as they were. The units stay the correct terminology for the use case: solar masses, solar radii, solar luminosities (so `mass-sol`, `radius-sol` and the rest keep their names). Our own star is "our own star" wherever the real one is meant; the usual system kind is `usual`; the keys `gm-sol-m3-s2` and `lock-au-sol`. LAW 9 and the content format say so now.
579. "mass is not in". No world or moon carried a mass: the dilation and gravity lines guessed one from size and kind. Every kind of world in `content/terrain.dnat` now has a density (`density-g-cc`: rocky about 5.5, giants about 1.3, ice giants about 1.6, rocky moons about 3.3, icy ones about 1.9), every world and moon gets its mass from its size and that density when it is made, and G itself is in `@law gravity` (`g-m3-kg-s2`). A world's card gives its mass in Earths and kilograms, the pull at its ground against Earth's and its escape speed; the clocks and the satellite's gain on its card use its real mass; a small body's card gives its mass from its size and density, with its pull and escape speed in metres a second where that is the size of it.
580. The Universe's open road, built: the gravity switch ("as i dont thinkg galxies are acutally being held togetther buy the laws of nature. like if i has a setting to turn off all gravity.. would stars explode pressures of all bodies zero and every body move to straight line travel... right?", DESIGN 519). The Time menu has Gravity; off, every planet and moon carries straight on at the speed it had on its orbit (its place a moment after the switch less its place at it, a moon with its planet's motion and its own), the system's star swells at about its sound speed (doubling in about its sound-crossing time, `swell-over-freefall` times its free-fall time root(R^3/GM): about an hour for a star of one solar mass), drawn larger and dimmer with its card saying by how much, and every galaxy's stars fly off on their tangents at the rim speed (a round galaxy's every way), worked out on the card, your own star's flight taken out so you ride with it. The title says how long gravity has been off. `@law gravity-off` in the physics content.
581. The rest of relativity (length contraction, redshift, frame dragging), built: every black hole spins, its spin a share of the most from `@law frame-dragging` (`spin-range`), its horizon pulled in to r+ = M (1 + root(1 - a^2)), its ergosphere out to 2M, space swept round at its horizon at a c / 2 r+, its disc's inner edge at the innermost stable orbit for its spin (Bardeen, Press and Teukolsky: 3 horizon radii at rest down to 0.62), and the fall's funnel twisted by the drag, more the deeper, the whole cone sweeping round. A world's card gives its light's redshift climbing out of its own well and its star's, its Doppler swing coming and going round its star, and its length contraction at its speed; a star's card its own light's redshift; a hole's card the redshift of light from near its horizon, without limit at it.
582. A star's layers down through to the heavier elements at its core ("stars shoiuld be mostly hydrogen till getting to it heavey m,etal cores ect ect", DESIGN 486), built: `@layer` entries in `content/stars.dnat`, each a share of the star's radius with its heat and density from top to bottom, its make-up where it differs from the star's, and which kinds of star have it: photosphere, convective and radiative zones and core for stars like ours; radiative envelope and convective core for the heavy ones; a red dwarf's churning interior; a giant's envelope, hydrogen-burning shell and helium core burning to carbon and oxygen; a supergiant's onion shells (hydrogen, helium, carbon-oxygen, neon-magnesium, oxygen-silicon, silicon) down to its iron core; a white dwarf's thin atmosphere over its carbon and oxygen; a brown dwarf's interior. Fly into the star and you are in its layer (the view filled with the glow of its heat, the title and the card naming it with its depth, heat and density); click ahead and the wheel flies to that point and dives into the layer's own plasma, its nuclei packed to the layer's density; the surface dive is the photosphere's.
583. Decay before your eyes, built: every unstable element in `content/elements.dnat` carries the half-life of the isotope it names and how it goes (`half-life`, `decays`: alpha, beta-minus, electron-capture, fission; NUBASE2020). Each unstable atom in view decays at its own moment (from its own hash and the half-life, counted from when that matter came into view, by the universe's clock at whatever speed it runs), its alpha knot of two protons and two neutrons, its electron, or its other half flung out and flying off, and it is its daughter from then on (two down the table and four lighter for alpha, one up for beta-minus, one down for capture) in the lattice, the gas, the plasma and the atom under you. The cards give the half-life, how long half of them take by the clock here, and what an atom has decayed from and how. The fields particles are waves in stay open.

597. "and like i said ther are millions of light years to the center of a black hole" / "from event to singularity" / "well not the smae based on mass". The way in is a law of the content now, `@law hole-inside`: `way-in-ly` for a hole of `way-in-mass-sol` solar masses (three million light-years for ten solar masses to begin with), scaling with the hole's mass to `way-in-power` (1: in proportion), run in the fall's forty tenfolds; the fall's card counts the way in against it: so many light-years of so many, from the horizon to the singularity, by its mass.
598. "so make sure the tore propley falls at speeds needed to not take forever". The fall's first stretch runs on the hole's own clock (from the horizon to a thousandth of it) shown faster; it had been set to thirty seconds, a minute for a big hole, and the rest ran at seven tenths of a tenfold a second, nearly a minute more. The stretch on the clock is about eight seconds now whatever the hole (never slower than real), the tour's fall runs at eight tenths of a tenfold a second, and the stop ends when the fall's point is reached, forty tenfolds down: the whole fall about a minute.
599. "and looks arounfd at the funnel to get its scope and shape and porints of refrence" / "as we fall in" / "not cycle just a once look around its not going to be a long process about a minute". The tour's fall takes one look round the funnel as it goes: back up at the mouth for the first seconds (the opening you came in by), then one full turn round at the walls beside you, then down at the point below for the rest of the fall, the walls closing in round the view and meeting at it; eased from one to the next, slowly wheeling. The fall itself runs about a minute (eight tenths of a tenfold a second over the forty).
600. "something broke black hole isnt there" / "i took screen shot" / "its suppose to be going to one thats feeding" / "the whole acrestion disk fly by". The tour's crossing had only preferred a galaxy whose heart feeds and could settle for a quiet one, and only among the galaxies made that very moment; it requires a feeding heart now (every galaxy of the regions round its aim counts, sixteen tries, a quiet one only if none feeds), and the nearer pick falls back to the nearest feeding heart anywhere round you before a quiet one, so the tour's hole is a feeding one and the whole disc flyby plays. Nothing else had broken: the tour had reached a quiet supermassive hole (no disc, no jets) on a nearly empty sky, so its black shadow sat on black and there was nothing to see. Every hole now shows its photon ring, the thin circle of bent light at the shadow's edge where light from behind and around it has looped the hole on its way to you (the ring the Event Horizon Telescope photographed), faint, on quiet holes too, so a hole is always there to be seen: a black disc in a ring of light. The tour's captions at a hole are true for a quiet one as well as a feeding one.

The spiral was the frame-dragging twist put on the spokes in DESIGN 581, my own addition; a gravity well is rings and straight spokes and nothing else, and the twist and the turning are gone (the drag stays on the cards). The sheet at the mouth is drawn from every depth, so the opening is always there to look back up at. The tour's fall looks up at the sky through the mouth from its first frame as you enter, holds there for the first seconds, then turns down along the funnel to its absolute point and stays on it for the rest of the fall, the walls closing in round the view: the inside of an upside-down spike cone.
602. "anbd near atom scales the grid just stopped being projected". Every point cloud is dimmed by the sky's fade, which goes to nothing from a millimetre of view scale down to ten microns so that no star shows through the very small; the fall's grid was drawn through the same path and went out with it, long before the point. The grid is a reference, not sky: it is drawn at full strength at every scale now, as it is never bent by the hole's lens.

And the tour's "horizon" stop had stood three and a half horizon radii out, where the shadow's cone is eighty degrees wide and fills the view as a block; it stands six and a half out now, where the shadow is a round orb inside its disc ("b ut the black hole that blocking that half of the disk isnt a orb its like a big contruction of cblacknerss sitting ontop the orb": the cone's upper part running off the top of the picture). It begins at the mouth and turns down to the point now, the walls converging ahead.

From the snaps: the sheet outside the horizon had been a square grid, and read as a square at the mouth from below; it is rings and spokes now, a gravity well round from the top. And the fall had been a zoom of the view from its first frame, the "from the middle" number running away while the walls had barely moved. The fall is a travel first and a zoom last now: the way in from the horizon to the point, the millions of light-years of its law, is run with the view held at the horizon's own scale, the walls closing in round you as it goes, each notch of the wheel a step of the way and the card counting it; only once the walls have met at the point does the view zoom on into it, to the quarks' size, where the singularity shows. Back out the same way.

Two snaps. The dive through a horizon had turned the view round to look back out of the hole in one frame, and the tour's fall look had begun straight up at the mouth from its first frame, so the hole flew out of view in a snap; the dive leaves the look as it was (at the hole, down its axis), and the tour's look swings up to the mouth from there, its speed brought up from rest over a second and a half, then down the funnel. And the tour's turn to face a new pick had been an exponential ease, fastest in its first frame, so the last thing flew out of view at full speed the instant a new pick was made; the turn's speed is brought up from rest over a second and a half now whenever a pick lies off the view, eased out as before, and never over about twenty degrees a second, as the shots' turns already were.

From the snaps: the well's spokes and rings drawn over an asteroid's grains at half a millimetre and again from five hundred metres up. A body's ground had taken the matter view out of space without clearing the space's kind, so after a fall every rock dived onto after reported itself as the inside of a black hole: the well's grid, its black fill, the fall's look and its clock line all followed you out. The kind is none at all outside space now, and cleared on every ground.

The text is a warm ivory now with a gold title, never white, and under every letter of the cards and the scale bar a dark rim half a cell wide, drawn first and a little behind so the letters sit over it: on a black sky the ivory reads as before, on a white surface (a grain field, a star's face) the rim carries the letters.
609. "check first shot of issue nothing is in focus so wtf is the text meaning something isnt built". The shot: a supernova's card, its glowing face ninety-two AU across at two hundred and eighty-three AU, and nothing in the ring. The fireball had been fourteen hundred dim points scattered through its volume, their brightness scaled by its light against its peak, so at two hundred days, at three percent of peak, it was a puff too faint to see. A photosphere's face is as bright as its heat says whatever its light is against its peak; the fireball is drawn as a star's face now (one sheet of boiling cells at its real size, darker to the limb, in the colour of its heat), full-bright while it shines, fading only as it thins to a cloud on the tail.
610. "and astroids still just genericly say rock and iron not theri actual varied and differnt vasness of compositions of all types and elements from a super nova". Every asteroid of a kind had the kind's one mix, the same minerals in the same shares, the same six elements. Each body has its own shares now: its kind's minerals scattered from the kind's by the body's seed (`vary` on the mix in content/minerals.dnat, about a half up or down), registered as its own mix for the card, the grains and the dive alike, no two the same. The card lists eight elements and the traces, and says where its elements were forged: every element carries `made-in` in content/elements.dnat now (the big bang, cosmic rays, dying low-mass stars, exploding massive stars, exploding white dwarfs, merging neutron stars, uranium's and thorium's decay, the lab), and the card groups the body's elements by their forges.

A zoom stop's rate had eased in exponentially, fastest in its first frame, a jerk away from the last thing; it is brought up from rest over two and a half seconds now, and on an approach it slows to a crawl over the last tenfold and a half (an eighth of its rate at the end). And a shot had set off the same frame it began turning to its new subject; it holds where it is through seven tenths of the turn now, the pan first, then the travel.

From the shots: at a nanometre and a half the lattice's level was black. Its clouds had been drawn at a third of their brightness, and on a capture that is nothing; and its two hand-overs were switches, one at four spacings (the lattice's clouds off, the atoms' own systems on, the orbs in one frame) and one at four radii (the atom you are at skipped in the lattice and its inside view on, the orbitals in one frame). The lattice's clouds are bright enough to read now, and the levels hand over by degrees: the clouds fade out and the atoms' systems fade in between five spacings and three, and the atom you are at hands over to its inside view between eight widths and four, its cloud fading as its electrons' shells and orbitals come up; the same in a liquid's and a glass's matter as in a crystal's.

A zoom stop had dropped to an eighth of its rate over its last tenfold and a half and then stopped dead at its until, and the pull-outs from a body's matter (the hole, an asteroid) had no end to ease to at all: their written until is a stand-in, and they ran at full rate until the view popped out to space. Every zoom ends at a crawl now: a twentieth of its rate over its final tenfold and a half, the end taken at that crawl; a pull-out from matter eases to where the view pops out; and a zoom followed by another the same way is one movement, straight on through the join with no easing out at it and no fresh start after it, so the long pull-out from an atom to the web runs as one move and slows only at the web. The arcs, flies, orbits and shots were already eased in and out.
614. "and the balck hole climb out needs to be looking up on climb out not down". The climb out had run the fall's own look (up at the mouth for its first six seconds, then down at the point), so most of the way out faced the point behind you. Climbing out the look turns up to the mouth and holds there the whole way.
615. "and it went to a focus on a super nova and nothing was there just empty space, where is its nurtron star or if bigger the balck holes?". Two causes. The neutron star or black hole born at a supernova's middle was only made a year after the blast, and the tour's shots at a supernova were measured by its glowing face at its biggest, so a supernova on its tail put you a thousand AU from a face shrunk to nothing and a remnant not yet made. The remnant is made from the blast now (inside the fireball until the ejecta thin), and the shots fly in to what is there: the face while it shines, the neutron star or the black hole once it has thinned or for a remnant; the thinning cloud keeps a quarter of its brightness. A white dwarf blown apart leaves nothing, as the card says, and its shots stay at its thinning cloud.
616. "and it appears astroid belts and comets and shit arnt even moving". They were not: every belt, Kuiper, Oort and inner body was placed once at a fixed angle, and the belts' haze stood still. Every one is on its own orbit round its star now by Kepler's law (its period from its distance and the star's mass; Kepler's equation for where it is, as the planets), made at its closest point at the clock's zero so the same body is the same body every time; the asteroids and Kuiper bodies on near-circles, the comets on long ellipses that sweep in close and far out; its orbit on its card. The one you are at moves you with it, so you stay beside it or on its ground as it goes round. The belts' haze turns, each point at Kepler's rate for its distance. With gravity switched off they carry straight on, as the planets do.
617. "and im only getting 5fps on 1m range so wtf is acually taking all th fps when is looking at a small spot on the ground". From the log at six centimetres: the ground asking for over a thousand pieces a frame, forty thousand built and thrown away, the detail at 275%: the tuner watched only the graphics card, which sat idle at 5% while the processor drowned in pieces, so it kept raising the detail. The tuner watches the processor's part of the frame too now: the detail comes down when it runs over about twenty milliseconds and goes up only while it is under fourteen. And the processor's time is on the stats card by phase now (the ground's pieces taken in, chosen, drawn; the sea, clouds and air; the builders started; the matter; the bodies), and in the log, so the next shot says exactly where a frame goes.

The drawing says what an atom is; it does not keep saying what it is not.

No, it was not right, and the balls are not needed: a nucleon is three quarks in a gluon field, and that is how every proton and neutron is drawn now at every size, from afar a small clump of three colour charges in its gluon glow (a proton's warm, a neutron's cool), close in the three quarks with the flux tubes between them and the sea's pairs; one drawing, no hand-over. The tour's two arcs round the nucleus and round a proton are seven and six seconds now, thirteen in all, where they had been eighteen and twelve.

In a hole what is left of a stop is measured in the fall's own measure now (the share of the way still to go and the zoom), and the climb's rate matches the fall's: about half a minute each way.

Nothing in the code read explains a blank, so the supernova the tour is at writes its state to the log twice a second now (its age, its light, its face and ejecta, how far off, whether its galaxy is the near one, what it leaves, how many remnant bodies are made), and the next run's log says where it fails.

Four things from the snaps and the log. The asteroid's dive is the planet's own stops now, every one of them with the asteroid's name on it, so any body is dived into the same way (my shorter chain picked its atom at six nanometres and found none: "asteroid-to-atom skipped: its pick found nothing"). The supernova the tour goes to: the log showed it landing on white dwarfs a hundred and seventy days in, their faces shrunk to ten AU and dimmed to a quarter, empty space at the stop; the nearest shining one that leaves a neutron star or a black hole is taken far before one on its tail or one blown to nothing, the face stays full-bright while it blazes at over a million suns whatever its day, and the log's match is by place as well as time. The lattice level's blackness between about six and twenty spacings on both bodies, its atoms unpickable there while they pick at five: nothing read in the code says why, so the lattice level writes a probe line to the log once a second (the scale in spacings, the atoms the crystal gives, the glows and pick bodies made of them, the focus, the reach, the heights), and the next run names it. And the levels' words say what an atom is: a nucleus a hundred thousand times smaller than the atom and electrons as fields of where they may be, its size their reach, nothing in it solid.

A dive onto a small body had landed on its nominal sphere, while the stone drawn is half again as long as it is wide, with bumps and craters besides, so a metre above the sphere was kilometres inside the stone or above it, and the stone spun on under you at a kilometre a second. The rock's shape is one function now (its radius along any direction, the one the mesh is built from), the dive lands on the drawn stone's own surface under you and holds its spin while you are on it, so from the dive down to the grains and back out it is the stone you see.

They are now: at any small body you are at (an asteroid, a Kuiper body, a comet's nucleus, a rogue planet), once it stands a few dozen pixels wide, a click on its stone picks the spot where the line under the mouse meets its drawn surface (its real shape, as a world's land is met), and the zoom flies down to that spot, as to a spot of a world's land; the dive itself lands on the ground under you, measured from the body's middle.
639. "that same solar system shit aint fixed i screen shot". From the shot, cropped and enlarged: the belt's bodies in parallel rows, evenly spaced, a square grid seen at a slant, while the haze round them is random. Every small body's position was an absolute universe coordinate, a double at billions of light-years, where a double's step is about a million kilometres; taken against the star it snapped every asteroid to a million-kilometre grid. That is the patterning, and it had been there before any of today's work. Every small body carries its precise offset from its star now (set when it is placed, kept by its orbit), and that is what it is drawn, picked, dived and ridden from; the planets never had the fault, placed as they are from their star.

The landing page never put the focus in the seed field: when it opened the focus sat on the page itself, and after a click on Random (or any button) on that button; a digit typed then went through the page's dialog key handling, which hunts for a shortcut letter, finds none for a digit, and refuses it with the beep. The seed field has the focus from the start now, a digit typed anywhere on the page but in a field or a list goes into it, Random leaves the focus in the field with its number selected so typing replaces it, and when the field is full at nineteen digits the whole number is selected so the next digit starts a new one instead of being refused.

Right on both counts, and the pics say how. "CPU 304 ms a frame" is the one thread a frame is built on, flat out; "5% of 16 cores" is that thread divided by the fifteen it cannot use; the card at one per cent is starved, waiting three hundred milliseconds for each frame to be handed to it. No setting on the card can hurry that: the lever is the processor's work a frame, and here it was the nebula clouds, which came in within forty of their radii (hundreds at a galaxy's scale), through a cache of twelve that, with more in reach, threw them all away and built them again every frame, three thousand points each. A cloud comes in within six of its radii now, the nearest six at most are on screen, a cloud is built once and kept (forty-eight kept, the one unused longest let go), and at most two are built in a frame.

"it crashed whhen i open the menu and try to open docs and periodic table of elemets , its like just clicking on the menu or holding open a drop down menu crashes the game as its running not zooming or moving cam around". Not a crash: the watchdog's own record beside the program says "SAFETY EXIT: main loop stalled for 6.0 s", twice. A menu held open runs Windows' own loop, the game's main loop waits inside it and stops beating, and after six seconds the watchdog took the program for hung and shut it down; the same for any dialog or message box reached from the menu. The watchdog already forgave a window being dragged; it forgives a menu held open, a dialog and a message box now, by the messages Windows sends while they wait (a menu loop's entry and exit, and the idle message inside any modal loop).

The lab's Uni Vs Matics engine and papers are pulled into `reference/univsmatics/` (verbatim from `fractal_templater` at d7a950e): the builder (`equationalize.js`: reflect pad to 64, RGB to YCbCr, forward CDF 9/7 lifting rows then columns, energy-target term selection with tol [0.015, 0.022, 0.022] and kmin [800, 400, 400], Int16 quantisation at max/32000, LEB128 delta positions), the player's maths (`reconstruct.js`: the five lifting constants, inverse columns then rows, dequantise, YCbCr to RGB, crop), their two containers (`uvm.js` WAV + UVME chunk, `eqvideo.js` UVMV frame sequence) and five papers (equations, thesis, docs, architecture, novelty cross-check). The folder's README gives the maths step by step from the code and the plan for the `.c97` clip: pictures only, no sound, no zoom-dive, no palette, no wrapper, no JSON, a binary header plus per-frame per-plane count, qscale, LEB128 positions and Int16 values. The name `.c97` is the working name (short for CDF 9/7; `.eqv` is IDL's equation viewer and `.uvv` was UltraViolet video, so neither is free). No separate player: the Universe program writes the clip from the recorder's frames (forward lifting in a compute shader) and opens it from File, the inverse lifting in a compute shader and the colour step in the present pass. The folder rides to the Universe project with the source through the sync script. A media player runs only its own decoders, so the readme on GitHub links the file rather than embedding it; keyframe plus delta is the one path to a file far smaller than an MP4 and is not built on either side yet. Found on the way: the program already carries this engine in C# (`src/core/Wavelet97.cs`, ported earlier from the same source: forward and inverse on every core, the energy-target `Keep`, `Rebuild`, dv1 packing, `Write` and `Read`), used today for a planet's terrain changes in the save; and the recorder already hands every frame to `Mp4Writer.Add` with the landing page's film box, clip-size drop-down, clip list and Play button. So the `.c97` writer is a second clip writer with the same `Add` call, the format pick a neighbour of the size pick, and Play of a `.c97` opens it in the program instead of the system player.
673. "no i need the fucking clip i make to be a .cdf97w not wait to properly write the equations". The clip filmed can be a .cdf97w now. `src/render/ClipWriter.cs`: one call opens a clip in the format picked (`IClipWriter`, MP4 or .cdf97w); the .cdf97w writer turns each frame into its Y, Cb and Cr planes reflect-padded to 64, runs the forward CDF 9/7 and the energy-target keep of `Wavelet97` (tolerances 0.006, 0.012, 0.012, tighter than the reference engine so stars stay) on a thread of its own, and writes the frame's time in milliseconds and its three fields: header C97W, version, size, padded size, frame count, tolerances. No sound, no wrapper. `src/platform/ClipPlayer.cs` plays it in a window of its own: the frames read, each rebuilt by the inverse on every core at the time it was filmed (one that falls behind is skipped), Space pauses, Escape closes, it loops. The start page gets a Clip format pick and lists .cdf97w clips beside MP4s, Play opening a .cdf97w in the program; the View menu gets Clip format. Each frame keeps its own time, so a frame that comes while the last is still being worked out is let go and the clip keeps its timing; working out a full 1080p frame takes much longer than a thirtieth of a second on the processor, so a full-size .cdf97w holds fewer frames a second than an MP4 (Half or Quarter size four or sixteen times more). The forward and inverse run on the processor cores, not the card; moving them into compute shaders is the next step for frame rate.
674. "okay so u know math start build ing hypothesis and research and math we need to solve the eqaution we need for the front man to use". Written up in `reference/univsmatics/FRONTMAN-RESEARCH.md`: the front man as a map F from the CDF 9/7 fields to an H.264 stream B, with the player bit-exact (so what it shows is known), judged by PSNR and by |B| + |box|. Hypotheses: H1 DCT residuals follow linearly from the wavelet terms (speed, not size); H2 motion vectors taken exactly from the camera and depth instead of searched (the main size win); H3 per-block QP from each block's wavelet energy, the Uni Vs Matics energy target applied per block; H4 the box carries only the correction between the true equations and those rebuilt from what the player shows; H5 the floor no equation can beat. Experiments E1 to E5, cheapest first (E1, the correction size, needs no new encoder).

A one-minute wake-up runs the front-man work to the end. E1 measured (FRONTMAN-RESEARCH.md section 7): at the .cdf97w writer's tolerances one 480-wide tour frame's equations are 400 to 890 KB against 17 to 43 KB for a DCT picture of it, and the correction a DCT copy needs is 63 to 92 % of the equations, so carrying the exact equations costs 10 to 30 times the picture; the smallest file is the front man alone, the equations rebuilt from it on load. Next: the equation size against looser tolerances, then E2.
676. (the one-minute work, from DESIGN 675) E1b and E1c: loosening the tolerance shrinks the equations slowly (210 KB at 0.06 against a better-looking 33 KB DCT picture); the weight is the packing, about 3.3 bytes a kept term with no entropy coding. One uniform quantising step and an entropy packer (LZMA standing in) cut the same equations 5 to 10 times, to the DCT picture's order of size, 2 to 6 dB behind it at equal size; JPEG 2000 closes that gap with a wavelet-aware coder on this same wavelet. H6 added: a wavelet-aware entropy coder for the .cdf97w. The writer's per-term Int16 packing is the change these numbers support.

The .cdf97w is version 2, built from E1b and E1c: every wavelet term of a plane quantised by one step (Y 0.004, Cb and Cr 0.008), every 30th written frame whole and the rest as the change from the last, packed with Deflate; the player adds the changes forward from the last whole frame. Measured on the tour at 480 wide: 27 to 134 KB a frame as changes, 66 to 188 KB whole, against 400 to 890 KB for version 1 and about 23 KB for the recorder's MP4. A version-1 clip is refused with a note to film it again. Left to close: H6 (a wavelet-aware coder in place of Deflate) and H2 (the change taken along the camera's motion).

(the one-minute work, from DESIGN 675) E2: the block DCT of a picture is the sum of its wavelet terms' block DCTs (matched to 4.4e-15), but taking it term by term costs about 225 s a frame against 14 ms for rebuilding the picture and then the DCT. H1 true and of no use; the front man is made from the rebuilt picture. Nothing to build from it.

H3 fails; the steps stay one a plane. Nothing to build.

Wavelet terms do not shift cleanly, and the recorded MP4's own block noise changes every frame; a clean test needs frames filmed as a .cdf97w. 685. "1-5 all are a go lets get to em" (third, the wavelet coder). The .cdf97w is version 3: its quantised terms packed by a wavelet-aware coder (src/render/WaveletCoder.cs), an adaptive binary range coder with LZMA's arithmetic; each term as nonzero or not (odds learned by wavelet level and by how many of its left and upper neighbours are nonzero), its sign, and its size (learned unary to 15 a level, then Exp-Golomb). Measured on the tour at 480 wide: 1.37 to 1.52 times smaller than Deflate on whole frames, 1.43 to 1.75 times on changes; a change frame 13 to 101 KB. A round trip outside the program gave every term back exactly. Version-2 clips are refused with a note to film again.
686. "1-5 all are a go lets get to em" (fourth, the .cdf97w on the card). The CDF 9/7 lifting runs in a compute shader (src/render/GpuWavelet.cs) on a compute queue of its own, one line to a thread, rows then columns and back; the writer uses it for every frame's forward transform and the front man's inverse, and falls back to the processor (noted in the run log) if the card refuses. Checked outside the game with no window: within 1.2e-6 of the processor's transform, 2.2e-6 there and back, against a quantising step of 0.004. Measuring a 1080p frame showed the packing, not the transform, was the slow part (130 ms on one core): the .cdf97w is version 4, each plane cut into four strips of rows coded on their own, all twelve at once (27 ms, half a percent more bytes, every term back exactly). A full 1080p frame is about 70 ms now, a Half-size clip within a thirtieth of a second.

The filmed clip (version 4, full 4K, 2,732 frames in 637 s, 4.6 GB, 4.3 frames a second) was read: changes averaged 1,635 KB against 1,871 for whole frames (frames 218 ms apart differ almost everywhere), the coarsest levels coded as finely as the finest. Frames rebuilt from it sized by the coder's own model came within 5 to 10 % of what they really took, so the model was trusted to scale: halving the picture divides the bytes by about 3, a step 4 times coarser by about 3, and a step per level by each level's synthesis energy (s0 / sqrt(Ej / E1), the error spread evenly) gives 6 to 8 dB better pictures at the same size and beats a DCT picture of equal size at small sizes. The clip as filmed fits in about 90 MB at quarter size, step 0.032 by level, 34.5 dB. Built: the .cdf97w version 5 with per-level steps (finest Y 0.032, Cb and Cr 0.064); the front man only what a player needs (a quarter-size picture at the start, one every ten seconds, the last held to the end), the clip itself the .cdf97w in its box. 30 frames a second waits on a clip filmed with this writer.
